# Chess Trainer test cases

> **Ids here are the REPO suite.** `TC-Rnn` and `US-Rnn` in this file mean the gate-executed Review
> suite and nothing else. A different 30-story / 31-case suite in the claude.ai project uses the same prefix for
> different cases, so a bare "TC-R05" is ambiguous outside this file. See `claude/stories/README.md`, the id-space
> register, before quoting an id anywhere else.

One case per acceptance criterion in USER-STORIES.md. Each case names the gate that executes it and the
build it was last executed against; a case with no gate is not a case, it is a wish. Results are copied
from gates/logs/<N>-all.log by the SAT pass, never typed from memory.

Columns: id · story · steps · expected (measured) · executed by · last result.

---

| TC-R09c | US-R37 | stepping ONE ply at a time into the forced-mate tail of the Opera Game (plies 30, 31, 32, 33), at 375x730, 375x679 and 390x844. A MutationObserver is installed on the document before each click and records EVERY value `[data-ct="eval-bar-num"]` takes, so `seq[0]` is the FIRST label painted on the new ply. That first label is the stored-analysis fallback BY CONSTRUCTION, not by luck: `sfHit` is keyed on `sfEval.fen === dispFen` and the live search is a worker round trip, so it cannot resolve in the same commit as the ply change. | THREE assertions, all over the first paints. (A) no value the element ever took is the clamped `+-9.9`. (B) all four first paints match `^(1-0\|0-1\|-?M\d*)$`, so (A) cannot be satisfied by an empty or unpainted readout. (C) at least one first paint is a BARE `M`, which only `_fbMate` can emit - `mateLbl` always appends a digit for \|m\|>=1 and otherwise returns a result, and the other two producers are gated on `engOn`, which is off here. THE EXACT MATE LABEL IS NOT PINNED [#391], and (B) is deliberately scoped to the first paint rather than to every sample so it cannot redden on whatever the live search happens to return. | 20-review.js `mate-tail` | FAILED BEFORE THE FIX on the REAL shipped bundle rather than a hand-broken one [#432]: `CT_APP=<git show 8b108fb:app.js>` (md5 8d8785ea40c4, stamp '#488 - 2026-10-06 17:33 ET') gives **150 PASS / 9 FAIL**, all nine being these three assertions at all three geometries. On #489 (md5 17c338a181d9): **159 PASS / 0 fail**, with first paints reading `M, M, M1, 1-0` IDENTICALLY at all three geometries. **THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS CASE POLLED SIX TIMES AFTER EACH CLICK AND ITS GREEN WAS NOT EVIDENCE [R18].** #489's diff-door antagonist measured a run of the FIXED bundle where ply 30 returned `M2` on all six samples - and `M2` cannot come from the fallback - so that ply collected ZERO paints of the branch under test, and had all four plies behaved so, BOTH assertions would have gone green on the #488 control. The polled version reddened the control 6 of 6 and the observer version reddens it 9 of 9, deterministically; the difference is that polling can miss a value that exists for one frame and an observer cannot. Assertion (C) exists because of that finding and is the 'assert the state was reached' rule [#385/#394] applied to this case. NOT COVERED AND NAMED SO: only the mate shapes this one fixture reaches - a mate in 2, two mate-in-1s and a mate on the board. There is no M3 or M7 and no position at a genuine +9.9 evaluation, all of which the job's own `case` asks for and all of which remain owed. Only the eval pill and bar; the coach chip and move-line are other render sites this case does not read. AND ONLY THE STOCKFISH ANALYSIS BRANCH: this gate pins `store:{ct_pool:'3'}` and never drives the no-Stockfish path at chess.jsx:4053, where `evalPawns` reaches +-99 only at a DELIVERED checkmate, so a forced-but-undelivered mate stores an ordinary material eval there and neither the defect nor the new label arises. That is the #375 'cover every branch' gap, named rather than closed. IF A LATER BUILD CARRIES MATE DISTANCE THROUGH `review.analysis` - the remaining half of jobs/review-mate-label-not-plus9-9 - the fallback will legitimately begin printing `M2` here and assertion (C) must be REVISITED rather than deleted. |

## Home - executed against #425 (gates/regress/49-home.js; log gates/logs/425-all.log, copied to claude/agents/gatelogs/425-all.log)

ID SPACE: the Home lane's TC-HM-001 … TC-HM-046 are defined in `claude/stories/HOME-LANE-2026-09-15.md`, which is
not in this repository; TC-HM-047 … TC-HM-050 were added by that lane inside `gates/regress/49-home.js`. They are
NOT a transcription of the other forty-nine (`R17`, and the `TC-R05` collision is what happens when a second copy
is hand-kept).

**CORRECTED 2026-10-10 [R18]. This paragraph read "Only the three rows below are recorded here, at the numbers the
gate actually emits" — and the second half of that sentence was false of the first half.** `TC-HM-047`, `TC-HM-048`
and `TC-HM-049` are numbers the gate actually emits: `grep -oE "TC-HM-[0-9]+[a-z]?" gates/regress/49-home.js` lists
all three, each as the message of a live `L.say` in block `HM-F` of `gates/regress/49-home.js` (three consecutive `L.say` calls; cited by block and symbol rather than by line, per the arm (4) ceiling).
So the suite has been asserting three Home cases that this register did not record, for as long as the note saying
only three were recorded has been here — which is `jobs/register-join-and-input-count-wrong-2026-09-28`'s own class
(a register that cannot be joined to the suite) in the direction that hides coverage rather than inventing it. The
three rows are added below. Every figure in them is read from the gate's own source, never from this note.

| id | story | what it asserts | pass condition | selector | inputs | failed before the fix |

Both of 050c's preconditions are asserted separately and both earned it inside this build: the first version of its
back-button selector matched an aria-label of `Back` belonging to a different sheet, the tap never reached Home, and
**precondition 2 went red rather than letting 050c pass by never entering the state it names.** That is the
`#385` rule working as designed — assert that the state was actually reached, and that the thing under test was
present just before — and it cost one subset run of seven minutes instead of a false green.

## Cross-app invariants - executed against #423 (gates/regress/26-invariants.js; log gates/logs/423-all.log, copied to claude/agents/gatelogs/423-all.log)

Population, stated once because every count below depends on it: a control is button, input, select, textarea,
[role=button], a[href], [onclick], or an element whose computed `cursor` is `pointer` - and cursor is INHERITED,
so an element in the SVG namespace and any control that has a control ancestor are both excluded and both
counted. React attaches click handlers at the root, so a div with onClick and no cursor:pointer is NOT covered;
that is a stated hole. The board is excluded by mechanism (the widest div whose inline gridTemplateColumns
matches `repeat(8,`) because its squares take taps and its pieces are drawn inside transform:scale(1.06).
Geometries: 320x568, 375x730, 375x568. Twelve screens, listed in the gate's SCREENS table.

**TRACEABILITY OF THE SIX TC-INV RESULTS BELOW IS UNVERIFIED, AND THIS IS THE REGISTER HALF OF THE FIX [R18, R45].**
Measured 2026-10-03 at `f3ae36a`, not read: `grep -oE 'TC-INV-[0-9a-z]+' claude/agents/gatelogs/423-all.log | sort -u`
returns **nothing** — zero occurrences across the whole log — and the same grep on `422-all.log` is also empty. So the
`PASS #423` cells on TC-INV-01, TC-INV-02a, TC-INV-02b, TC-INV-02c, TC-INV-02d and TC-INV-03 **cannot have been copied
from the cited log by id**; they were transcribed from the gate's narrative output. That breaks the rule this file states
in its own header — "Results are copied from gates/logs/<N>-all.log by the SAT pass, never typed from memory" — and the
rows added at #423 break it in the same commit the rest of the register was audited in.

**THE CLASS IS WIDER THAN THE SIX ROWS THIS NOTE WAS WRITTEN FOR, AND THE COUNT IS STATED RATHER THAN THE WORD
"SOME" [R06, R07].** Swept mechanically over the whole file at `f3ae36a` on 2026-10-03, one row per case id, matching
BOTH id shapes in use here (`TC-R01` has no hyphen before its number, `TC-INV-02a` does — a first pass of this sweep
used a pattern that required the hyphen, silently dropped every `TC-R` row, and reported 2 traced out of 23; that wrong
figure is recorded here because it is exactly the error this note is about): **61 case rows; 47 publish a result against
a build whose `-all.log` is committed; 18 of those 47 are traceable to their log by id and 29 are not; 14 more cite no
build at all.** Traceability is therefore **38.3%**, and the untraceable 29 are TC-R18, TC-R19, TC-R20, TC-R30, TC-R37,
TC-R43, TC-R44, TC-R45, all twelve TC-INV, all seven TC-PL, TC-SUITE-007 and TC-BN-001. The traceable 18 are TC-R01–R14,
TC-R16, TC-R35 and two TC-HM rows — i.e. almost exactly the families whose gates carry the id in the assertion text.
That is the correlation the fix rests on: rows are traceable where, and only where, the gate emits the id.

The six TC-INV results are therefore **recorded as claimed-but-untraceable rather than deleted**: the gate may well be green,
and deleting a row for a gate that is actually green would be the more expensive error. What is missing is the mechanism,
not the measurement. THE MECHANISM ALREADY EXISTS IN THIS REPOSITORY and is what makes the Review rows checkable — the
same grep for `TC-R[0-9]+` on `claude/agents/gatelogs/373-all.log` returns TC-R01 … TC-R14, because `20-review.js`
carries each case id as a literal prefix in its `L.say` messages. 26-invariants.js does not.

THE REMAINING HALF IS NOT IN THIS FILE AND WAS DELIBERATELY NOT TAKEN HERE: giving each `L.say` in 26-invariants.js's
`inv2`, `inv2-pins`, `inv2-pinset`, `inv2-population` and `inv3` blocks its TC-INV id prefix, and the verifier step in
`gates/verify-log.sh` that asserts every id whose row claims a PASS at build N occurs in `N-all.log`. Both live in files
this run did not hold a lock on [R44, one agent one artefact], so they stay open on
`jobs/tc-inv-case-rows-cite-a-result-that-is-not-in-the-log-2026-09-27` and
`jobs/nothing-checks-that-the-registers-citations-resolve-2026-09-30`. Until the gate emits its ids, no SAT pass can
refresh these six cells, and the correct action on them is to re-measure, never to re-type.

TC-R35  US-R25, the Review list's month independence and its stated cap.  INPUTS, 11 (6 concentrated + 3 THIN + 2 RETURNING-USER, the last five added #432): a stubbed chess.com archives index of 24 monthly archives (2024-10..2026-09) in which 2026-04..2026-08 hold 60 games each (300 "in older months") and 2024-10..2026-03 hold none, seeded three ways on the newest month - (a) 0 games, (b) 5, (c) 60 - crossed with 1 and 3 connected accounts. Per-account totals 300 / 305 / 360.  PASS CONDITIONS, each its own assertion so one cannot absorb another: (i-A1) on seed (a) the list is NON-EMPTY; (i-A2) on EVERY input the list holds rows dated OUTSIDE the newest month, read off the row's own date label in the DOM - THE ASSERTION THE DEFECT IS ABOUT; (ii-A3a) rows == accounts x min(total seeded, the cap), where the cap is the number the app STATES on screen, falling back to 200 when it states none; (ii-A3b) a cap of at least 200 is stated on screen as readable text matching /(?:up to|at most|limit)\s+<number>\s+games/i; (iii-A4) measured localStorage footprint after the fetch is under 4,000,000 chars, and (A4b) the footprint projected at the cap across all 8 account slots is too; (A5) the rows on screen after a reload with the network refusing equal the rows on screen before it - the quota catch at chess.jsx:232 swallows a failed write, so a cap raised past the budget looks right and is gone on the next launch; (A6a/b/c) at 320x568, 375x730 and 440x956 no game row runs off the screen horizontally, the first row is hit-testable at its own centre after its scrolling ancestor is scrolled, and the "N loaded" count is inside the viewport.  SELECTOR: [data-ct="game-row"], grepped from chess.jsx:5724.  GATE: gates/regress/61-review-list-month-independence.js.  MEASURED ON #427 (90abeba, app.js md5 95fa1c00fcc89ef9f9dce3d46061a703): 33 pass / 20 fail over the 6 inputs; A2 red on ALL SIX, A1 red on both seed-(a) inputs; exactly TWO requests are issued per account, the archives index and archives[last], so 22 of the 24 months are never asked for. On a phase-one candidate bundle: 57 pass / 0 fail.  #432 THE THIN INPUTS, AND WHY THE FIRST SIX COULD NOT FIND THE DEFECT: all six concentrate 300 games in the five months immediately before the newest, so the backward walk ALWAYS reaches ACCT_GMAX and stops on GAMES. The month exit was unreachable, and this gate went 42/42 green over a bundle that named the wrong limit in it. THREE MORE INPUTS, every month equally thin: (T1) 24 months x 5 games = 120 per account, at 1 and 3 connected accounts, where ACCT_GMONTHS binds at 6x5 = 30 rows per account; (T2) 3 months x 5 = 15 per account, where the archive index runs out and NOTHING binds. 5 games a month rather than the job's suggested 3 because 24x5=120 is the footprint antagonist B actually measured the defect on.  PASS CONDITIONS: (A7-0) vacuity - the stub was requested and a list rendered; (A7a) rows == accounts x (ACCT_GMONTHS x per-month) on T1 and == the whole account on T2, which is what proves WHICH bound stopped the walk and is the precondition for the rest; (A7b) on T1 the screen states the SIX-MONTH window; (A7c) on T1 the screen does NOT state a games cap as the bound, since the games cap is not what stopped it - this is the defect itself and is the assertion that has to be read off the BODY text to be able to fail; (A7d) on T2 no limit line element is rendered AND no limit sentence is anywhere on screen, which is also what returns the 24.8px that the sentence cost the shortest screens; (A7e) the limit line meets WCAG AA (>= 4.5:1) against everything painted behind it; (A7f) the "N loaded" count beside it does too - pre-existing, swept here rather than left [R06].  MEASURED ON THE SHIPPED #431 BUNDLE (f419760, app.js md5 feaa5eb17669623ea45674b39640c155), which is the negative control and cost nothing to obtain: 10 FAIL - A7b x2, A7c x2, A7d x1, A7e x2, A7f x3 - each naming its own cause (located line "Showing up to 200 games per account." with months on screen and 30 of 120 rows; the line present at 16.8px where nothing was cut; 4.37:1 and 3.75:1). On the #432 bundle (md5 b2d952e1011f): 16 pass / 0 fail over the three inputs.  TWO INSTRUMENT ERRORS WERE CAUGHT BY THIS CONTROL AND BOTH ARE WORTH KEEPING: (1) A7b/A7c/A7e first read [data-ct="games-cap"], an attribute #432 itself adds, so on #431 they saw null - A7b went red for a MISSING SELECTOR rather than for wrong wording, and A7c, "the screen does not state a games cap", went PASS on the one bundle where it does. A check that reads only what the fix added cannot see the defect the fix removes. They now read the body text and locate the line by what it SAYS. (2) the contrast ratio first composited against the nearest OPAQUE ancestor, rgb(20,22,27), and returned 4.51:1 - above the 4.5 threshold, so A7e could not fail - while the antagonist's report and a hand calculation both said 4.37. The effective background is rgb(25.7,32.6,41.9): one translucent layer sits between, and compositing it reproduces 4.37 and 3.75 exactly. The threshold sat BETWEEN the two readings, so the wrong instrument would have shipped a contrast fix with no assertion able to see it.  #432 THE RETURNING USER, TWO MORE INPUTS AND THE ONLY ONES IN THIS CASE THAT DO NOT FETCH. #432 records which bound stopped each walk in its own key (ct_acctcap), so an account imported by an EARLIER build has rows in ct_acctgames and no entry there - the state every existing player is in on the launch after they upgrade. All nine inputs above create their store BY FETCHING inside the same session, so none of them could see it, and the first version of #432's render filtered on the missing entry and drew NO limit line at all for those players - dropping the statement US-R25 requires and that #431 got right, until they re-fetched. Found by asking what the fix does to a store it did not write. INPUTS: ct_accts and ct_acctgames seeded directly with (R1) 200 rows, at the cap, and (R2) 12 rows, below it; ct_ccuser left UNSET so the auto-fetch at chess.jsx:3689 does not fire.  PASS CONDITIONS: (A8-0) the stored games render from localStorage with no request; (A8a) at the cap the games cap is STILL stated, because an account sitting at ACCT_GMAX was cut by ACCT_GMAX and that much IS inferable from the old store; (A8b) below the cap NOTHING is stated, because months and exhausted are indistinguishable from an old store and a limit the app cannot know bound is not one it may claim.  MEASURED: on a #432 bundle built WITHOUT the fallback (md5 e95884e803b6), A8a FAILS with stated=NONE over 200 rendered rows - the regression as it would have shipped; A8b passes there, so the control is specific. With the fallback (md5 f7229b6ad3cb): 37 pass / 0 fail over the whole gate.  #433 TWO MORE INPUT SHAPES, AND THE FIRST OF THEM IS THE ONE THE FIXTURE ITSELF WAS HIDING. Every index above is a CONTIGUOUS run of months, which is not what api.chess.com returns - it lists only months that HAVE games - so the fixture encoded the same assumption as the code and no assertion here could see that ACCT_GMONTHS counts ENTRIES. (S1) SPARSE: 8 archive entries at 2023-11, 2024-01, 2024-06, 2025-02, 2025-09, 2026-03, 2026-08, 2026-09, 5 games each - antagonist B's own measurement reproduced. The 6-entry window takes 30 rows spanning 2024-06..2026-09, a CALENDAR span of 28 months. PASS CONDITIONS: (A11-0) vacuity, the sparse index was requested and a list rendered; (A11a) rows == 6 newest ENTRIES x 5, which is what proves the entry window is what bound; (A11b) the fixture separates the two quantities - the measured span exceeds the window, so a units error is REACHABLE here and is not on any dense fixture; (A11c) THE UNITS, pinned to the DOMAIN rather than to a wording - any calendar claim of N months on screen must cover the span actually shown, which is false by 4.7x on #432 whatever words it uses and stays true of any future wording; (A11d) the bound IS stated, in months of play; (A11e) no games cap is named, 30 rows never reached it; (A11f) no gap is claimed when every requested month answered. (S2) GAP: 3 contiguous months x 5 with the MIDDLE month answering 503, so the index exhausts and the only reason the count is short is the failed month. PASS CONDITIONS: (A12-0) vacuity AND the proof the hole is real - 10 rows where an unbroken walk gives 15; (A12a) the screen SAYS a month could not be loaded rather than rendering nothing and thereby claiming the whole history is here; (A12b) it names NO limit, because neither bound was reached; (A12c) AA on that sentence too. ONE MORE ON THE LEGACY STORE: (A10c) the NUMBER stated is the cap that actually cut that account, asserted against the row count on screen rather than a constant this gate holds - A10a asks only whether a sentence is there, and that is how #432 came to print "up to 200 games" over a store cut at 40. MEASURED, with the #432 branch bundle as the negative control and it cost nothing to obtain (source 3def71d rebuilt, md5 97ab409e00b4): 7 FAIL - A7b x2 (monthsOfPlay null under 'last 6 months'), A10c (stated 200 over 40 rows), A11c (claimed 6, measured 28), A11d, A12a (no line rendered at all on a dropped month), A12c - each naming its own cause. On the #433 bundle (md5 0355154e0050): 96 pass / 0 fail. #433 AFTER THE ANTAGONIST PAIR, FOUR MORE INPUT SHAPES AND ONE MORE ASSERTION, EVERY ONE OF THEM A STATE THIS CASE COULD NOT ENTER. (S3) LEGACY BAND: stores of 200, 137 and 41 rows with no ct_acctcap, beside the 40 and 9 already here. ct_acctcap arrives in #432 and #432 has never shipped, so the bound-less population was written by builds up to and INCLUDING #431 - which is on main with a cap of 200 - and 41..199 is the band the deployed app creates. (A10c) the number stated is the cap that actually cut that account; (A10b) NOTHING is stated at 137 and 41, because a row count is not a cap and this app has shipped exactly two. (S4) EMPTY MONTH: a listed archive that answers 200 with an empty games array, which chess.com's index says cannot be empty - (A13a) the screen says a month could not be loaded, (A13b) and names no limit. (S5) LEGACY BESIDE MONTHS: a 40-row legacy store beside a fetched account the month window bound, which no earlier input crossed - A9 gives both accounts a recorded bound and A10 runs the legacy store alone, so the union was never asked to choose. (A14a) the games cap stated is the CURRENT one, not the legacy cap of the one account it stopped; (A14b) the legacy cap is still named as the qualifier; (A14c) the month bound is named too. (A11g) with one account the line occupies ONE line box at 375x730, pinned against the element's own lineHeight rather than a pixel count. (A11h) THE ASSERTION WITH DOMAIN CONTENT: the months-of-play claim EQUALS the distinct calendar months on screen, asserted where no gap is claimed - A11c can only fire on a calendar claim the shipping bundle never makes, and A11d checks only that the number is 6. TWO CONTROLS, BOTH REBUILT HERE: the #432 source (md5 97ab409e00b4) 99 pass / 13 FAIL - A7b x2, A10b x2, A10c, A11c, A11d, A11h, A12a, A12c, A13a, A14b, A14c; and the FIRST #433 candidate (md5 0355154e0050), which is the more specific control because it differs by three defects only - 106 pass / 6 FAIL: A10b x2 (it printed 41 and 137 back as caps), A11g (the sentence ran to two line boxes), A13a (an empty month was silent), A14a and A14b (the union named 40 over 70 rows). On the shipped #433 bundle (md5 c9e9640e88b5): 112 pass / 0 fail.

TC-R45  US-R16 clause 5 (#471), every control that would replace a live game asks first, and the SAME control must be tapped twice.  GATE gates/regress/70-live-game-reset-guard.js.  INPUTS, 46 assertions at 375x730 and 37 at each of the other two (blocks J, K and K3 are state assertions with no viewport term and run at Kunal's geometry only, which is said rather than sold as coverage [#411]) = 120 measured states: geometries 375x730 (Kunal, R19), 320x568 and 390x844; CONTROLS DRIVEN = the More sheet's 'New game' row, two of the twelve Game-setup pills the menu paints over a Pass & Play game end to end plus all twelve measured for movement, and the lesson practice sheet's 'Play this position vs Computer'; GAME STATES = 3 plies live (Pass & Play 1.f3 e5 2.g4), 4 plies FINISHED (fool's mate), 0 plies fresh, and 0 plies with a POSITION LOADED through Review -> 'Play from here' -> 'Play this position'.  PASS CONDITION, per input and never aggregated: (a) one tap on a guarded control leaves the ply count and the move row unchanged AND the control says it is armed; (b) a second tap on the SAME control performs the reset; (c) a second tap on a DIFFERENT control leaves the game and moves the arm; (d) no control moves by more than 0.01px, measured CONTENT-ABSOLUTE, between the unarmed and armed states; (e) with a fresh board one tap applies immediately AND no warning row is rendered at all; (f) Rematch over a finished game still works on one tap; (g) on a LOADED position at zero plies the 64-square occupancy string is unchanged by one tap and the sheet says so.  SELECTORS: every pill and sheet row is located BY ITS OWN RENDERED TEXT inside the pre-existing [data-ct="menu-sheet"]/[data-ct="more-sheet"], never by a data-ct this build added.  IT FAILED BEFORE THE FIX, measured rather than quoted: against the REAL SHIPPED #470 bundle (md5 ec59168f03a7) 24 PASS / 19 FAIL at 375x730, every ARRIVAL assertion green so the reds are measurements and not missing selectors - B1 plies 3 -> 0 on one tap of 'New game', C2 3 -> 0 on 'No clock', F1 4 -> 0 over a MATED game with F1b recording the result text going to [], J1/J2 the loaded position destroyed with the sheet saying nothing, K1 the lesson route destroying a 3-ply game.  EIGHT CONTROLS, each reddening a different block: NC-MAIN as above; NC-PLIES (the pre-veto playHist predicate) reddens J1 and J2 ALONE, which is what isolates the predicate change - and NOT K, because that control changes the predicate and not the ROUTING, so the lesson route it leaves wired through guardReset is still guarded by a 3-ply game's own ply count. K1's control is NC-MAIN, where the routing does not exist. I had written "the J and K blocks" here BEFORE running it, which is the one thing this project punishes hardest; the control was run and the sentence corrected [R18]; NC-BOOL (a boolean arm instead of a key) reddens D1/D2 alone; NC-DEADARM (apply() removed) reddens the 'second tap works' assertions while the 'does not destroy' ones stay GREEN - #469's D2 lesson; NC-NORESERVE (row rendered conditionally, not reserved) reddens C4 at 35.04px of pill movement against a 0.01px tolerance; NC-LONGLABEL (a More-sheet armed label long enough to wrap) reddens B2 and B4 and NOT B3, which is the opposite of what I predicted when I built it - so B3 STILL HAS NO CONTROL, and the measurement explains why: the More sheet's rows are FIXED HEIGHT, so a longer label cannot move the rows below it at all. B3 is therefore a standing guard against a mechanism the sheet's own layout already prevents, not a live discriminator, and it is recorded as that rather than as covered; NC-NODISARM (the disarm effect deleted) is I1/I2's; NC-ALWAYSROW (the row rendered unconditionally - this build's own first candidate) is E2's, measured at 42 pass / 1 fail with E2 the single red. AND K3, THE ASSERTION THAT THE LESSON BUTTON STILL COMMITS ON ONE TAP WHEN THERE IS NOTHING TO LOSE, exists because the fix for the antagonists' veto BROKE IT: `game` is shared across modes and holds the LESSON's position in learn mode, so an unscoped board predicate armed that button every time, demanding two taps and printing 'discard your game' with no game in existence. Measured on the veto-fix candidate before it shipped; neither antagonist found it, because it was in the code written in answer to them. The predicate is now scoped by mode.  WHAT NO CONTROL COVERS, said rather than implied [#411]: B3 and C4 are GREEN ON NC-MAIN BY CONSTRUCTION because nothing ever arms there, and C5/I1 are vacuous there for the same reason - each names its own control above.  Logs: claude/agents/controls/471-NC-*.log, 471-fixed-all-geos.log.
TC-R46  US-R16 clause 6 (#474), the Play setup sheet must not offer - or warn about discarding - a game that another mode has already replaced.  GATE gates/regress/71-resume-row-agrees-with-the-board.js.  INPUTS: 24 assertions x 2 geometries (375x730 Kunal's phone per R19, and 320x568) = 48 measured, over 6 browser sessions (a live-game block, a mode-switch block and a coincidence block at each geometry). ROUTE, driven and not read: Pass & Play 1.f3 e5 2.g4 (3 plies) -> the house -> the Play tile -> read [data-ct="setup-resume"] -> resume -> Home -> Discover -> Openings -> Italian Game (selectOpening, which calls setGame directly) -> the lesson's ✕ -> the Play tile -> read the row, [data-ct="play-moverow"] and [data-ct="setup-start"] again.  PASS CONDITION, BOTH HALVES, because neither is evidence alone [#469 D2, #471 rule 1]: over a game that IS live the row is PRESENT (A2), its stated count EQUALS the highest move number the move row prints (A3, the #385 cross-check rather than a regex), one tap on Start ARMS (A4) and tapping the row returns the game WITH its moves (A5); once the board is no longer that game the row is ABSENT (B1), the move row is empty (B1b) and Start applies on ONE tap without claiming there is a game to discard (B4).  FOUR ARRIVAL ASSERTIONS AND A VACUITY SENTINEL, and both earned their place by catching this gate's own first draft: A2a/B2a assert the setup sheet was actually reached (my first probe printed "no disagreement" for three doors where the navigation had failed, so the row was absent because the screen was never there - #385's rule); B2z asserts window.__ctLive survived, because a page reload destroys playHist and would make every reading vacuous (measured: gates/drive/puzzles' `train` state DOES reload); A4a/B4a assert the Start button was scrolled on screen before being tapped, because ▶ Start game sits below the fold at 320x568 and without them B4 PASSED ON THE BROKEN BUNDLE there - a tap that misses produces no arm, so "does not demand a second tap" was satisfied by a button never pressed.  BLOCK C, THE COINCIDENCE, ADDED AFTER AN UPHELD ANTAGONIST VETO AND IT IS THE REASON THIS CASE IS WORTH READING. Blocks A and B pair a 3-ply live game with a 0-ply lesson board, so their two ply counts can NEVER collide - and this build's first predicate was LENGTH EQUALITY, which every inherited board of a matching ply count satisfies. Antagonist A broke it by changing ONE number: a 2-ply live game and a lesson PRACTICE played out to 2 plies. The fixture had encoded the same assumption as the code [#432/#433], so block B could not fail on that input class. Block C drives exactly it, with C0/C0b/C0c asserting the live game, the lesson arrival and the sheet first. THREE BUNDLES, and the middle one is what makes block C evidence rather than decoration: the SHIPPED #473 (96c21b94df25) 40 PASS / 8 FAIL, red at B1, B4, C1 and C2 at both geometries; the LENGTH-ONLY candidate this build first wrote (gates/.trial/app-474-lengthonly.js, md5 7794c779b309) 44 PASS / 4 FAIL, red at C1 and C2 ONLY with block B entirely GREEN - which isolates the predicate change and proves C discriminates the coincidence and nothing else; and the shipped identity predicate 48 PASS / 0 FAIL. THE C1 READING ON THE LENGTH-ONLY CONTROL IS THE WHOLE DEFECT IN ONE LINE: resume "▶ Resume your game in progress (1 move played)" against a move row reading "1.e4 1…e5" - the LESSON's moves, not the player's f3/e5 game. Logs: claude/agents/controls/474-NC-473-shipped.log, 474-NC-LENGTHONLY.log, 474-fixed-identity.log.

TC-R47  US-R35 (#475), a drill goes BACKWARDS as well as forwards and a card already solved comes back solved.  GATE gates/regress/72-drill-prev.js.  INPUTS: 2 drills (Your mistakes and Your brilliant moves - two entry points, startMistakes and startBrilliant, so both are inputs and not one plus an assumption) x 2 geometries (375x761 = Kunal's phone per R19, and 320x568) = 4 runs, each seeded with a FIVE-card queue and walking 1->2->3 then 3->2->1 with a solve on card 2 = 16 card transitions plus 4 inert-at-the-front taps. 100 assertions.  PASS CONDITIONS, the four limbs: (a) a Prev control exists, is enabled on cards 2..N, is PAINTED but disabled on card 1, is distinguishable there (opacity < 1), is inside the viewport and is REACHABLE BY A FINGER; (b) Prev decrements the index by exactly 1 and the counter reads the new index in both directions; (c) a card solved on the way out is not asking again when revisited and still shows its verdict; (d) the drill has exactly ONE visible control that leaves it.
  IT FAILED BEFORE THE FIX against the REAL SHIPPED BUNDLE rather than a hand-broken one. NC1 = origin/main #474, md5 8b958354102b: 4 PASS / 8 FAIL, red on A1 (prev is null) and A3 (exits:2, the two identical '< Review' buttons) at both geometries, independently reproducing sit run 19's #437 measurement four days later. The walk cannot run there at all, and the gate says so in a note rather than reporting a harness error.
  NC2 ISOLATES LIMB (c), which is the half a button-existence check cannot see. A trial bundle (md5 46d4d0a04173) keeping Prev and removing ONLY the solved-state restore measures 92 PASS / 8 FAIL, red on A10b and A10c alone in all four runs and green everywhere else - so (c) is independently controlled and does not ride on (a).
  A9b IS THE POSITIVE CONTROL FOR (c)'s INSTRUMENT. 'Already solved' is read as 'the Hint row is gone and a verdict is on screen', both DOM facts rather than colours, because chess.jsx renders that row under `{!puzSolved&&...}`. A9b asserts that a card which was never solved reads as ASKING, so the instrument is shown able to return both answers before A10 is believed; without it, A10 passing would be consistent with a gate that simply cannot find a Hint button.
  A2e EXISTS BECAUSE THE FIRST RUN OF THIS GATE SCORED 66/6 ON ITS OWN TAP. At 320x568 the drill control row rests at y 490..542 while the FIXED tab bar occupies 512..568, so elementFromPoint at the row's centre returns the tab bar and a naive click never reaches Next. It is NOT unreachable: #root is the app's scroller (overflow-y auto, scrollHeight 612 vs clientHeight 568) and 44px of real finger scroll clears it, measured by actually scrolling and re-reading the rect. A2e now asserts that reachability and PRINTS the scroll it needed, so a build that makes the row genuinely unreachable reds here instead of being scrolled around. The row's resting position under the bar is pre-existing - the same row carried '< Review' + 'Next >' before #475 - and is filed separately rather than folded into this change.
IT FAILED BEFORE THE FIX, against the REAL SHIPPED #473 BUNDLE (md5 96c21b94df25) rather than a hand-broken one: 40 PASS / 8 FAIL, red at B1, B4, C1 and C2 at BOTH geometries, every arrival assertion green so the reds are measurements and not missing selectors. The row read "▶ Resume your game in progress (2 moves played)" over a move row reading EMPTY, and Start armed with "Tap again to discard your game and start" about a game that no longer existed. After the fix: 48 PASS / 0 FAIL. Block A is GREEN ON BOTH BUNDLES AND MUST BE - the affordance works on #473 for a game that really is live; block B is what separates them, which is said rather than left to be inferred [#411].  NOT COVERED, stated as the gap it is: the PUZZLE and REVIEW doors, which also call setGame and are this job's own open notChecked items. Driving the Puzzles tile reached the roadmap WITHOUT replacing the board - the move row still read "1.f3 1…e5 2.g4", so the probe never exercised the class and its "no disagreement" says nothing - and the puzzles driver reloads. The fix is keyed on the board rather than on any one door so it covers them BY CONSTRUCTION, and a construction argument is not a measurement.  R36 ADMISSION: deterministic across the two runs taken at the time of writing (34/0 both, and 30/4 both on the control); the third is the full-suite run. Runtime ~110s for all four sessions.  Logs: gates/logs/474-all.log (suite section), plus the control run in the #474 run report.
  BLOCK B IS THE INPUT CLASS BLOCK A COULD NOT SEE, AND IT WAS ADDED AFTER TWO BLIND ANTAGONISTS FOUND THE
  SAME DEFECT FROM OPPOSITE DOORS. Block A seeds five rows that are ALL playable, so 'the raw queue index' and
  'the position among the playable rows' are the same number in all 100 of its assertions - and the first #475
  build confused the two. puzzleFromMistake refuses a saved row whose position or stored move is illegal (that
  guard is why such rows exist), and with one at the FRONT the drill opened on '2 of 5' with Prev ENABLED at
  opacity 1 and completely dead, while the build's own comment claimed 'disabled and visibly inert'. Block B
  seeds the three unplayable shapes the guard itself names - no stored move, an illegal stored move, and an
  illegal position - so 2 of 5 rows are playable, and asserts the counter counts what can be SHOWN, that Prev
  is disabled and dimmed on the opening card, that Next steps over the gap, and that Next leaves only at the
  true end. MEASURED: the pre-fix bundle (8abd4061b66a) scores 124 PASS / 14 FAIL, red on B1, B2, B4, B5, B6,
  B7 and B10 at BOTH geometries, reading counter {i:2,n:5} and prev {dis:false,op:1}. The fixed bundle
  (f53b69654395) scores 138 / 0. The fix was to hold the PLAYABLE ROW INDICES as the one source of truth,
  which settles the dead Prev, the overstated counter and the premature exit together [R09].
  A10d/A10e ASSERT THE BOARD AND NOT THE CHROME, and that gap was real: antagonist A built a bundle whose
  restore painted the WRONG POSITION under a correct-looking verdict and it scored 100 PASS / 0 FAIL against
  the first version of this case, because A10b/A10c read only 'is the Hint row gone' and 'is a verdict there'.
  The restored position is now compared piece for piece against the position the live solve produced, with A8b
  as its control (the live solve must actually move a piece, or 'restored == solved' would be satisfied by a
  board that never moved). Control NC4, chrome restored and board not (8f8714019066): 130 PASS / 8 FAIL, red
  on A10d and A10e ALONE in all four runs.
  A11d IS LABELLED A TRIPWIRE rather than counted as coverage: mistakeQueueRef.current is assigned in exactly
  two places, both from a .slice(), and never mutated in place, so 'the queue length did not change' cannot go
  red for any build that does not edit startMistakes/startBrilliant.
  THE 320x568 RESIDUE, STATED RATHER THAN HIDDEN: the drill's nav row rests at y 490..542 with the fixed tab
  bar at 512, so the button's upper 22 of 52 px are tappable with no scrolling (a real click at top+6 moves the
  counter) and its CENTRE returns the tab bar. Pre-existing and byte-identical on #474; #475 makes the slot
  load-bearing, which is why it is filed at priority 9 as
  jobs/the-drill-nav-row-rests-under-the-tab-bar-at-320x568-on-main-2026-10-04 rather than fixed here - the
  fix reserves the bar's height and trades against board height on phones already at the 192px floor, which is
  an open Kunal decision.

## Play - game over, executed against #434 (gates/regress/16-cpu-result-line.js; log gates/logs/434-all.log, copied to claude/agents/gatelogs/434-all.log)

TC-PL-031  US-PL-11, the outcome is still stated after the result card has gone.  INPUTS, 4: (1) vs Computer (Pip, 500 Elo), resigning as White at move 0, at 375x730; (2) the same at 320x568; (3) vs Computer at the Elo FLOOR - ct_elo seeded 400 and no bot tapped, so chess.jsx:3143 computes ne = max(400, 400-50) = 400, equal to cpuElo, chess.jsx:3144 never fires and eloMsg stays empty - at 375x730; (4) Pass & Play, resigning as White at move 0, at 375x730, which is the auditor's own control.  PASS CONDITIONS, each its own assertion: (A1a/B1a) the resignation puts a result card up reading Resigned; (A1b/B1b) and WHILE THAT CARD IS UP the status slot carries the strength note - asserted BEFORE the absence below, so a green cannot come from eloMsg never being set (#385); (A2a/B2a) ~6.6s later the card has gone; (A2b/B2b) and the slot states the result as the app's own two-part string, head + " · " + sub, matched EXACTLY rather than by an alternation any wording satisfies (#388); (A3/B3) the outcome named is the one the GAME had - this side resigned as White so the player lost, which is a fact about the game and not a reading off a sibling of the thing under test (#389); (A4/B4) a scan of every painted element whose own text is under 44 chars and matches /won|win|lose|lost|Time!|Resigned|Checkmate|Stalemate|draw/i finds the result on screen, head/style/invisible excluded; (A5/B5) the slot is painted, opacity 1, not present-and-invisible; (A6/B6) its scrollWidth fits its clientWidth on a nowrap ellipsis span, so nothing in it is cut; (A7/B7) the board's width and top are identical either side of the fade; (A8/B8) zero app errors.  (C1) at the Elo floor no strength note appears at all; (C2) the result appears in the slot regardless; (C3) and no strength note is there to pre-empt it - this input was ALREADY CORRECT on #433 and stays green on both bundles, which is what localises the defect to the pre-emption; (C4) zero errors.  (D1) Pass & Play states "Resigned · Black wins" in the same slot, naming the WINNER rather than a first person that does not exist in a two-player game; (D2) the scan finds it; (D3) zero errors.  SELECTOR: [data-ct="play-opening"], which is #370's and predates this build - so a red here is wrong TEXT and can never be a missing hook (#432's trap, avoided by construction rather than by care).  GATE: gates/regress/16-cpu-result-line.js.  MEASURED ON THE SHIPPED #433 BUNDLE, which is the negative control, is the real broken build and cost nothing to obtain (bc6f3e7, app.js md5 c9e9640e88b5, stamp "#433 - 2026-09-29 09:03 ET"): 21 pass / 6 FAIL - A2b, A3, A4 at 375x730 and B2b, B3, B4 at 320x568, every one of them reading "▼ Strength now ~450 Elo" where the result belongs, or an empty hit list. C and D green there, as designed. On the #434 bundle (md5 39195e453d01): 27 pass / 0 fail.  NOT COVERED AND NAMED SO: LANDSCAPE. `wide` (chess.jsx:2744) is true at 730x375 and chess.jsx:5616 does not render this slot AT ALL when a game is over and wide, so at 730x375 NEITHER opponent states a result after the fade - measured on the #434 bundle, vs Pip and Pass & Play alike, the scan returns []. TC-PL-033  US-PL-11 clause 7, the landscape board is the same size whether the player LAUNCHED in landscape or ROTATED into it, and it is the size that screen's own geometry allows.  *(Added #436, from jobs/landscape-board-shrinks-8px-on-a-rotation-round-trip-2026-09-29, found by #435's antagonist A. WHY THE ROUTE IS THE WHOLE POINT: gate 16's blocks E and F were this repository's ONLY landscape coverage and they `L.launch` DIRECTLY at 730x375, which is a state no phone can be in - a real device is opened in portrait and turned. So every landscape number this suite published described a viewport no user enters, and it happened to describe the one value that was an artifact. The instrument here is the round trip.  PASS CONDITIONS, per geometry: (0) the viewport really is that landscape size - a harness self-check, not a claim about the app; (1a) a board is painted on the fresh launch, so a green below cannot come from there being no board (#385); (1b) rotating to portrait really did change the board (216 -> 353.03), which is the control that makes the return leg mean anything - without it the round trip could be a no-op and every equality below would be trivially true; (1) THE FRESH AND ROTATED BOARDS AGREE IN WIDTH AND TOP to within 0.05px; (2) a SECOND round trip leaves it unchanged, separating a stale value from one that walks; (3) the settled board equals vp.h-24-130 floored to a multiple of 8 - SQ's own wide branch (chess.jsx:2752) with trim 0 - stated per row as 216, 232, 216 so a reader can see why three rows expect three different numbers rather than taking them on trust; (4) zero app errors across two round trips.  PLUS TWO THAT SPAN THE GEOMETRIES: (A4) the three FRESH widths must not all be one value - three screens whose available heights are 221, 236 and 221 cannot correctly produce one board. This is the assertion that needs no knowledge of the right answer, is pinned to no literal, and survives any later change to the 130px chrome budget; (A5) the portrait play board at 375x730 is still 353.03 - GREEN ON BOTH BUNDLES by design, because it is the control proving the fix stayed inside `wide`, portrait play running through the `_edge` path where the trim is real (#364 measured 56px of slack it reclaims).  NOTE ON A1-A3's SHAPE: they assert equality between TWO MEASUREMENTS OF THE SAME BUNDLE, not against a literal, so they cannot be made green by moving a constant. TC-PL-034  US-PL-12, a finished game keeps its finished-game controls when the player steps back through it, and offers nothing enabled that cannot act.  *(Added #437, from jobs/the-control-row-is-keyed-to-the-ply-shown-not-to-whether-the-game-is-over-2026-09-29, found by #436's auditor. Filed as the CAUSE, not an instance: R09, because four register items share this mechanism.)*  INPUTS, 2 geometries x 3 terminal kinds = 6 end-to-end drives, plus 1 instrument-validation drive per geometry = 8: at 375x730 (Kunal's phone) and 320x568, each of (B) checkmate by 1.f3 e5 2.g4 Qh4#, (C) resignation at 2 plies through the More sheet's two-tap arm, and (D) a threefold-repetition draw by knight round trips, every one in Pass & Play from ▶ Start game.  WHY THREE TERMINAL KINDS AND NOT ONE: `_gameOver` has two terms and they are reached by different endings. Checkmate sets NO playEnd, so it exercises the getStatus(game) term alone; resignation and the draw set playEnd and exercise that term alone. A case driving only checkmate would leave half the predicate untested, which is #375's single-worker-fallback mistake. It also earned its keep immediately: it is what MEASURED that the defect was confined to the getStatus half, so resignation and the draw were never broken — see the control counts below, where all ten reds are in block B.  PASS CONDITIONS, each its own assertion.  FIRST, THE INSTRUMENT, because every assertion about the dead control is an ABSENCE claim and this repository's rule is that an absence is worthless until the instrument is shown able to move: (A1) at 2 plies of a live game the row IS the live row, Hint and Flip present — the state the later comparisons are against; (A1b) the 64-square signature reads exactly 64 squares; (A2) TAPPING HINT IN A LIVE GAME MOVES THAT SIGNATURE. If A2 fails, every "the Hint does nothing" below is vacuous and the gate says so in the assertion text rather than in a comment.  THEN, per terminal kind: (x1) ARRIVAL — the game really ended and the result card names this ending, so a green cannot come from a game that never finished (#385; the first draft of this gate went 14 red for exactly that reason, because Pass & Play needs ▶ Start game and the moves were landing on a setup sheet); (x2) at the terminal ply the row offers Review and Rematch; (x3) ARRIVAL — stepping Back twice really changed the position, asserted by the board's own signature differing from the terminal one, without which every assertion below is silently about the terminal ply; (x4) THE DEFECT — Review and Rematch are STILL in the row after the step back; (x5) the live-only Hint has not reappeared; (x6) the row is UNCHANGED, same labels in the same order; (x6b) and no control moved horizontally, max dx asserted at <=1px and measuring 0; (x7) no enabled board control in the row is inert, tested by tapping each and requiring the signature to move — the check A2 has already proved capable; (x9) ARRIVAL — the More sheet opened on the stepped-back finished game; (x10) and it does NOT offer Resign, because resign() also returns on getStatus(game) and it would be a second inert control; (x8) zero app errors through the ending and the step back.  SELECTORS, and this is deliberate rather than careful: the row and the sheet are located by the LABELS `_CBtn` and `_SheetItem` have always rendered (title= and the label span), NOT by any data-ct this build adds. #432 shipped three assertions keyed to an attribute its own commit created, and against the one bundle where the defect was live the selector returned null and the assertion that the defect was ABSENT went PASS. A check that reads only what the fix added cannot see the defect the fix removes; here that trap is avoided by construction, because every selector predates the change.  GATE: gates/regress/63-gameover-chrome-keying.js.  FAILED BEFORE THE FIX, against the real shipped build rather than a hand-broken one, which costs nothing and cannot be accused of being built to fail: `CT_APP=<#436 app.js read out of origin/main, md5 8d3cb851039170a77901389cd550483e, stamp "#436 - 2026-09-29 16:54 ET"> node gates/regress/63-gameover-chrome-keying.js` gives **60 PASS / 10 FAIL of 70** — sixty, not the 62 an earlier draft of this case published, and the correction is #437's antagonist A's: the log's own footer reads `GAMEOVER-CHROME-KEYING: 60 pass, 10 fail`, `grep -c '^PASS'` is 60, and the control runs 70 assertions rather than 72 because 6b is skipped behind a failed 6 [R18]. The ten are B4, B5, B6, B7 and B10 at each of the two geometries — the row rebuilt to [Moves, Back, Forward, Hint, Flip, More], inert:["Hint"] measured by the validated instrument, and the sheet reading [New game · Resign · Analyze · Copy moves]. On the #437 bundle: 90 PASS / 0 fail at md5 a2b4078d9fbd, the bundle that shipped. (An earlier candidate, md5 c21f8c8d9c92, gave 72/0 against a 72-assertion draft of this gate; that draft is superseded and its log is void — see THE REPAIR below.)  **THE REPAIR, AND IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT THING IN THIS CASE.** The sheet assertion in the first draft was VACUOUS and the build did not catch it; antagonist A did, with a control the build had not run. A took the shipped #436, renamed the item `Resign` → `Forfeit` and left the defect gating untouched, so the sheet still resurrected it on a mated game stepped back — and the assertion PASSED, because it tested `!labels.some(t=>/^Resign$|^Tap again to resign$/)`, a search for a fixed string that no longer existed. That is #432's trap running in the opposite direction: a check that reads only a string the fix did not change cannot see the defect when the string moves. There was no positive control anywhere for the sheet, where A2 does exactly the right thing for the row. REPAIRED as (9b) a POSITIVE CONTROL — the LIVE game's sheet, read at ply 0 through the SAME `sheetOf` selector, must offer a resign control, so a rename or a removal reddens HERE and names the cause — plus (10) a SET DIFFERENCE, `liveSheet \ overSheet` must be exactly one item and that item must match /resign/i, which is vocabulary-independent for the defect itself. Proved on THREE bundles: 90/0 on the shipped tree; 74/14 on the real #436; and **44/22 on A's `app436-renamed.js`, where 9b and 10 both go red** — the bundle on which the first draft was green. Note also (11) THE DECIDED BEHAVIOUR, and the story of that assertion is the most useful thing in this case. It began as a CAPABILITY assertion - Flip must be reachable somewhere on a finished game - added after antagonist B measured that the first draft removed the last route to it, and it was satisfied by adding Flip to the play More sheet. The FULL SUITE then went red on `46-play.js:274`, which pins that sheet's exact contents, and following the red led to `46-play.js:280`, which labels the whole question NEEDS-KUNAL K-PL-4. That label is stale: `decisions/q-play-flip-gameover` (Kunal, #390) reads "Flip a finished game in Review instead" with an empty note, so the route is the Review screen BY DECISION and the app's behaviour was right all along. The sheet item was reverted and assertion 11 INVERTED to pin the decided behaviour - Flip offered neither in the row nor in the sheet on a finished game stepped back - so the accidental route the old keying opened cannot return unnoticed. 46-play.js asserts the same property at the terminal ply; this is the stepped-back half, which that gate never visits. **A subset run of gate 63 alone would have been green for the wrong reason**, which is the sharpest argument in this file for why a subset never authorises a push. Blocks C and D are green on BOTH bundles by design, and that is not a vacuity but the measurement that localised the defect to one of the predicate's two terms.  NOT COVERED AND NAMED SO: STALEMATE, the other branch of the getStatus term — fixed by construction since it is the same expression, but not driven and therefore not measured; a FLAG FALL, which shares the playEnd term that was never broken but costs 56s of wall clock per geometry; vs-COMPUTER as the opponent, since every input here is Pass & Play; the ONLINE opponent, which takes a different panel entirely; LANDSCAPE at any geometry, and landscape with real safe-area insets, which #436's own handover note named as the most likely place for an escape against that build and which this case does not touch; more than two Back taps, and Back-then-Forward-then-Back; and the ten remaining `isOver` readings in the wide/landscape chrome, which are jobs/ten-isover-readings-left-keyed-to-the-previewed-ply-2026-09-29 and are left with reasons rather than half-fixed.

TC-PL-035  US-PL-13, a finished game in LANDSCAPE offers the finished-game controls and every one of them is tappable.  *(Added #438, from jobs/landscape-game-over-keeps-the-live-row-and-the-rail-clips-half-of-it-2026-09-30, found independently by BOTH of #437's antagonists from different doors and handed over on the pen. TC-PL-034 named "vs-COMPUTER as the opponent" and "LANDSCAPE at any geometry" among its NOT COVERED; this is those two gaps closed, and it is the same predicate one axis over.)*  INPUTS, 10 end-to-end drives: 3 landscape geometries x checkmate (730x375, 844x390, 667x375), plus resignation at 730x375, plus vs-COMPUTER resignation at 730x375 and 844x390 = 6 terminal drives, each paired with its own live-game drive at the same geometry and the same opponent for instrument validation = 10 browser sessions. Every landscape state is reached by driving the game in PORTRAIT and THEN ROTATING, which is gate 62's rule and not decoration: gate 16's blocks E and F published this repository's only landscape numbers for builds by launching directly at 730x375, a state no phone can be in, and the one value they described turned out to be an artifact.  WHY BOTH OPPONENTS AND WHY BOTH TERMINAL KINDS, and this is the assertion-design point of the case: `_gameOver` is `playEnd || getStatus(game)`, so checkmate exercises the getStatus term alone and resignation the playEnd term alone; and Pass & Play sets opponent==='human', so it NEVER mounts the three wide-only sites (the bot chip, the Elo pill, the Elo slider) that only exist at game over in wide. The FIRST VERSION OF THIS GATE WAS SIX PASS & PLAY SESSIONS AND WENT 57 PASS / 0 FAIL GREEN ON A TREE WHERE vs COMPUTER WAS STILL BROKEN - row bottom 253.5 against a clip of 242, 9 of 12 points on every control, persisting through a step back. That is #375's single-worker-fallback mistake exactly, a gate exercising one branch twice, and it was caught by measuring the configuration rather than by reading the diff.  PASS CONDITIONS, each its own assertion.  FIRST THE INSTRUMENT, because every claim here about a dead or absent control is an ABSENCE claim: (B0a) the 64-square signature reads exactly 64 squares; (B0/C0) TAPPING HINT IN A LIVE LANDSCAPE GAME MOVES THAT SIGNATURE, driven per geometry and per opponent. If these fail, every "no Hint" and "nothing inert" assertion below is vacuous, and the gate says so in the assertion text rather than in a comment.  THEN, per state: (B1/C1) the row offers Review AND Rematch; (B2/C2) and offers NO Hint, so the enabled-and-inert control is ABSENT rather than merely clipped; (B3/C3) EVERY control in the row is hit-testable down its WHOLE height, sampled every 4px, AND at its own centre - elementFromPoint and not scrollIntoView, because script can reach a clipped box and a thumb cannot (#380/#382); (B4/C4) THE MECHANISM - the row's bottom lies inside the rail's visible window (rect top + clientHeight), so nothing depends on scrolling a rail that shows no affordance; (C4b) at game over the wide-only Elo slider and Elo pill are NOT mounted, which is the 39px that pushed the row out vs the computer; (C4c) the bot name is stated ONCE and not twice, pinning the duplicate #435 acted on; (B5a/C5a) ARRIVAL - the Back control could actually be tapped, guarded so the assertions after it are not silently about the terminal ply; (B5b/C5b) all of B1-B4 hold again after stepping back.  AND THE TWO THAT STOP IT BEING SATISFIED THE CHEAP WAY: (B6a/C6) a LIVE landscape game STILL offers Hint and Flip; (B6b) and offers NEITHER Review NOR Rematch; (B6c) and its row is fully reachable, the baseline the terminal ply must match; (C6b) and it still hides the Elo chrome, so the pre-game/live half of that condition is untouched. Without B6/C6, keying the swap to a constant `true` would satisfy B1 to B5 and break every game in progress. (B7) the PORTRAIT regression guard: #437's portrait row still swaps at the terminal ply, so this build cannot fix landscape by breaking the phone.  **THESE SIX - B0, B0a, B6a, B6b, B6c, B7 and C0, C6, C6b - ARE GREEN ON BOTH BUNDLES BY DESIGN AND ARE NOT PART OF THE CONTROL COUNT.** 27 of the control run's 39 passes are them. That is stated because a reader counting reds needs to know which assertions could never have been red.  WHY NOT `scrollHeight === clientHeight`, WHICH IS WHAT THE JOB ASKED FOR: measured before it was encoded, and it is wrong as written. On the SHIPPED bundle the rail's scrollHeight exceeds its clientHeight by 142px in the LIVE landscape game and after every step back, with all twelve points on all six controls reachable - the excess is the moves-panel, and this project's own rule is that vertical spill inside a scroller is fine. So that assertion would go RED on a healthy bundle and would have to be weakened later, which is how an assertion becomes decoration. The job's proposed fix is a hypothesis, not a prescription; the property encoded instead is the one the player's thumb finds. The job is amended with the measurement rather than silently departed from.  SELECTORS: the row is located by the `title=` labels `_CBtn` has always rendered, the rail by its computed overflow-y and its own rect, the Elo chrome by what it SAYS (a range input, and a span matching the app's own "~NNN Elo"), the board by gates/lib.js's repeat(8,...) finder. Every one predates this build, so #432's trap - an assertion keyed to a hook its own commit adds cannot see the defect the fix removes - is avoided by construction rather than by care. (d) "A half-clipped Review" was false: the button was 100% inside the rail's painted window at bottom 210.5 against a clip at 242 - what was clipped was the six-button row BENEATH it.  **AND THE FIX CHANGED SHAPE TWICE AFTER THIS CASE WAS FIRST WRITTEN, BOTH TIMES BECAUSE SOMETHING OTHER THAN THE BUILD CAUGHT IT.** First, `16-cpu-result-line` E3 went RED at strengthHits:[] when all three wide-only sites were suppressed - E3 pins EXACTLY ONE painted element stating the computer's adaptive strength once the result card has gone, and #435 is the build that deliberately reduced it from two to one. Zero is not an improvement on two, so the Elo pill was restored. Second, antagonist A then swept landscape HEIGHTS - an axis nothing in this case had touched - and with the pill back ABOVE the row the defect returned: measured vs Pip resigned, headroom +21 at 780x380, +5 at 780x368, **-3 at 780x360 and -11 at 780x352, 730x350 and 711x320, where six controls drop to 83% reachable**. So the pill now renders BELOW the control row, which is US-PL-13 clause 4 applied literally, and the re-sweep gives **+24px minimum headroom with 100% reachability and 0 dead controls at all nine geometries**. THE RESIDUAL IS MEASURED AND FILED RATHER THAN LEFT: at heights at or below ~352 the pill itself is cut ~8px with its ink sliced (61% visible, its own centre still hit-testable, its `+` still acting 450 to 550), which is `jobs/landscape-elo-pill-is-sliced-below-352-the-residual-438-chose-2026-09-30` - a box this build CHOSE to make the one that gives, rather than a defect it discovered. And E3 itself counts a PAINTED pill without asking whether it is clipped, which is `jobs/gate-16-e3-counts-a-painted-pill-not-an-unclipped-one-2026-09-30`.  **COVERAGE, STATED AS A FRACTION RATHER THAN AS A SWEEP, because antagonist A counted it and the first draft of this case overclaimed:** the sentence "3 geometries x 2 opponents x 2 terminal kinds" describes 12 cells and **6 are driven** - (730,mate,PP), (730,resign,PP), (844,mate,PP), (667,mate,PP), (730,resign,CPU), (844,resign,CPU). The portrait regression guard B7 fires ONCE, at 730x375 checkmate Pass & Play, and nowhere else. **AND THE "mouse click AND element.click()" HALF OF THE INERTNESS CLAIM IS NOT IN THIS GATE**: `tapTitle` uses `x.click()` only. The mouse-click measurement was taken in an uncommitted probe, so a reader cannot re-take it from the artefact - stated here rather than left to look like gate coverage.  THE ASYMMETRY IN THAT TABLE IS ITSELF A MEASUREMENT AND IT CORRECTS THE HANDOVER NOTE: checkmate fails 6 and resignation 8 at the same geometry, because after a step back a MATED game un-clips while a RESIGNED one does not. `isOver` reads the previewed ply, so the 52px button unmounts when you step back from a mate; `playEnd` does not, so it stays. #437's pen note says "on #436 stepping back unmounts this button ... and the row un-clips" - true for checkmate, FALSE for resignation, where the clip is permanent for the rest of the game. Found by driving both terms of the predicate, not by reading the note.  NOT COVERED AND NAMED SO: the ONLINE opponent, which renders its own Review/Rematch/Flip/Leave panel in a different block and is left as its own job; STALEMATE and the threefold-repetition DRAW, which share `_gameOver`'s two terms with the two endings driven here and are fixed by construction but not measured; a FLAG FALL, same term, 56s of wall clock per geometry; landscape at any width other than 730, 844 and 667, and landscape SHORTER than 375; landscape with REAL safe-area insets, which #436's handover named as the most likely place for an escape and which headless Chromium cannot produce - antagonist B of #437 measured safe:'51,31' as giving numbers identical to safe:'' at every point; vs-COMPUTER ending by CHECKMATE, covered here only through the resignation path - antagonist A tried to drive it twice and Pip defended both mating attempts, then closed the gap by proxy instead: the LIVE vs-Computer landscape layout has the same class of chrome mounted and measures a row bottom of 200.5, identical to vs-Computer resigned, so the class re-mounting costs 0px of rail and the gap is benign rather than merely unmeasured; more than one Back tap on the vs-Computer path; and the remaining `isOver` readings in the wide chrome, which are jobs/ten-isover-readings-left-keyed-to-the-previewed-ply-2026-09-29 and are left with reasons rather than half-fixed.

TC-PL-036  US-PL-14, once a game has ended no touch on the board or on a pending promotion choice can add a move to it.  *(Added #439, from jobs/a-pawn-can-be-promoted-into-a-game-that-already-ended-on-time-2026-09-30, found by #438s auditor pass and measured by #438 as PRE-EXISTING on the #437 bundle - the identical probe gave a byte-identical verdict on both, which is why #438 filed it rather than reverting.)*  INPUTS, 14 browser sessions: the two CLOCK blocks at 375x730 only (each costs a real 60-second flag - measured 57.5s and 59.2s), plus resign-vs-Computer, resign-Pass&Play, mate and live-game blocks at 375x730 AND 320x568. Each terminal drive is paired with its own live-game drive in the same session for instrument validation. WHY THE INPUT SET IS SHAPED LIKE THAT, and it is the design point of the case: `_gameOver` is `playEnd || getStatus(game)`, and the defect is entirely in the playEnd term, so CHECKMATE is the counter-example rather than an instance - D1 must stay GREEN on both bundles, and it is what says the hole is in playEnd and not in the status check. RESIGN is in the set because the job says 'the clock is the only route in' and that is TRUE OF THE PICKER AND FALSE OF THE BOARD: resign is one tap from the More sheet of every game, needs no clock, and reddens C1 and C2 at both geometries.  PASS CONDITIONS, each its own assertion.  FIRST THE INSTRUMENT, because every claim here is an ABSENCE claim: (A0) the picker is UP with four ENABLED 52x52 buttons on the LIVE game before the flag, and (A0b) opening it commits no move; (B0) the same tap that will be refused later ADVANCES a live game; (E1b) a white queen is identifiable by its own image and a8 does not hold one yet. If these fail, every assertion below is vacuous.  THEN, per state: (A1) the picker is GONE once the result card exists; (A2/A2a) a tap where its queen button was appends no move and writes no promotion into the row; (A2b) the captured-material readout is unchanged by that tap; (A3) Rematch raises no picker on the fresh game; (B1) the board refuses a legal move after a clock flag; (C1/C2) the board refuses a legal move after RESIGN, vs Computer and Pass & Play, at both geometries.  AND THE FIVE THAT STOP IT BEING SATISFIED THE CHEAP WAY - by refusing the board, or the picker, for everybody: (D1) a MATED game refuses the board and (D2) carries the finished-game row, both GREEN ON BOTH BUNDLES BY DESIGN; (E1) a LIVE game still accepts a legal move; (E2a) a LIVE promotion still opens the picker; (E2) it COMPLETES and a WHITE QUEEN now stands on a8, identified by matching the image of the queen on d1 rather than by the move row, so the board and the notation are two independent readouts; (E2b) the promotion is written into the row; (E2c) the picker closes after a live choice. Without E1 and E2, returning false from the move guard unconditionally passes B1, C1 and C2 and breaks every game in the app, and never painting the picker passes A1 to A3 and makes promotion impossible - strictly worse than the defect.  GATE: gates/regress/65-promotion-after-gameover.js.  FAILED BEFORE THE FIX against the REAL shipped build rather than a hand-broken one: `CT_APP=gates/.trial/app-438-shipped.js` (md5 166a99dd02e5, stamp '#438 - 2026-09-29 23:24 ET') gives **38 PASS / 9 FAIL of 47**. The 9: A1, A2, A2a, A2b, B1, C1 and C2 at 375x730, and C1 and C2 again at 320x568. On the shipping #439 bundle: **47 PASS / 0 fail**.  **ONE ASSERTION IN THIS CASE CANNOT BE REDDENED BY THE SHIPPED BUNDLE AND THE FIRST DRAFT CLAIMED IT COULD [R18].** A3 was published as '#438: FAIL (stale picker survives fullReset)'. MEASURED: A3 PASSES on #438, and it must - there the A2 tap COMMITS the promotion and the handler's own clear runs, so no stale choice ever reaches Rematch. A3's control is a THIRD bundle, built for it rather than argued: the paint-only candidate gates/.trial/app-nc2-paintonly.js (md5 14905f6f0ce3, the render guard kept and the clearing effect deleted) scores 12 pass / 1 FAIL with A3 the single red, while A1, A2, A2a and A2b all go green. That is the whole argument for the fix's shape, and it is a measurement rather than a preference.  A SECOND SELF-CAUGHT ERROR, IN THE INSTRUMENT RATHER THAN IN A CLAIM: E2's first two drafts measured NOTHING. Draft one counted a queen GLYPH across the 64 squares - but pieces are `<img src="data:image/svg+xml;base64,...">` inside a transform:scale(1.06) wrapper, so a square's innerText is its rank/file LABEL and the count was 0 on every bundle, an assertion that could not pass. Draft two used elementsFromPoint - but the piece images sit under pointer-events:none so the square takes the tap, the helper returned null for BOTH squares, and `a8After===wq` became null===null, a vacuous TRUE. E1b is the assertion that caught the second one and is in the gate for that reason. The third version computes the square's child index from the board's own flip flag, because Pass & Play flips to whoever is to move and a fixed index is wrong after every ply.  NOT COVERED AND NAMED SO: the CLOCK blocks (A0..A3, B1) run at 375x730 ONLY, because each flag is a real 60 seconds of wall clock and two geometries would put this gate past two minutes on its own - the picker is position:fixed inset:0 and centred so it is geometry-independent by construction, but that is a reading and no claim in those blocks is a claim at 320x568. An ONLINE game: its branch already gates on og.status/og.result and the fix is a no-op there, but sign-in and egress are both blocked in this sandbox, so it is UNTRACED rather than passed. Promotion in a vs-COMPUTER game - the job's own notChecked item - is not driven; the picker is opened by the same single call site for every opponent, which is why it is not duplicated, but that is the mechanism and not a measurement. LANDSCAPE at any geometry: neither the auditor nor this gate drove 730x375. **THAT SENTENCE IS WITHDRAWN AT #489 [R18], AND SO IS THE '14 browser sessions' FIGURE ABOVE IT.** #488 added BLOCK H, which drives 730x375 for the Pass & Play picker-at-a-clock-flag path (12 assertions, gate header lines 81-89), so this case document has disagreed with its own gate since 2026-10-06T23:27Z. #488 fixed the gate-side half of the antagonist veto that produced block H and recorded in its own pen note that it had left this half undone and owed; this run held the lock on this file anyway and took it, which is the only reason it closed - it was nobody's job. WHAT IS NOW DRIVEN AT 730x375: block H only. STILL NOT DRIVEN AT 730x375, quoted from the gate's own header rather than re-derived here: the vs-Computer path, the RESIGN/MATE/LIVE blocks C/D/E, the drag block G, and A3. AND THE vs-COMPUTER CELL IS NOT MERELY UNDONE, IT IS BARRED: `chess.jsx:189` adds `Math.random()*randomness` to the bot's choice, so a drive of that cell is not reproducible and R36 refuses it until there is a seeded engine or a fixture. THE SESSION COUNT IS DELIBERATELY NOT RESTATED AS A NEW NUMBER. '14' was true at #439 and is now wrong; the authoritative count is the `65-promotion-after-gameover` section of the full-suite log, and replacing one stale literal with another literal that the next block to land will stale again is the frozen-denominator mistake this project records at #405. The #414 auto-draws by repetition and fifty-move, which set playEnd by the same path as resign and are fixed by construction but unmeasured. And whether the appended move corrupted a subsequent Review or Analyze beyond displaying it, which is the job's own last notChecked item and is still open.    **EVERYTHING FROM HERE WAS WRITTEN AFTER THE ANTAGONIST PASS, WHICH VETOED THIS BUILD ON TWO GROUNDS AND WAS RIGHT ON BOTH [R18].** The case above is left as written rather than edited, because what it got wrong is the useful part.  (1) **THE FIX WAS INCOMPLETE AND THE INPUT SET IS WHY.** Every move in every block above is driven with the harness's `b.move()`, which is tap-then-tap - two `mouse.click` calls - so THE POINTER IS NEVER DOWN ACROSS A STATE CHANGE and the whole DRAG half of the board's input space went unexercised. `onPtrUp` reaches `commitOrPromote` WITHOUT re-checking `humanCanMove` (only `onPtrDown` calls it), so a piece picked up while the game was live and released after the ending still committed: measured at 375x730, Pass & Play, 1 min, the clock flagged with the piece held, the card read 'Time! White wins', and the release appended '3...Nf6' with 0 console errors. NEW BLOCK G drives a real press-drag-release across a flag; G0 proves the drag commits in a live game first, and G1 is RED on shipped #438 (plies 4 -> 5, '3.Bc4') and green on the shipped fix. THE REPAIR IS THE SHAPE OF THE FIX, NOT THE COUNT: the guard moved to `doMove` itself, the one place every route lands, so 'how many routes are there' stops being a claim that has to be re-audited each build. The classSwept figure of 'two sites' published on the job is therefore WITHDRAWN - it was an absence claim made by grepping `doMove`'s call sites, and it missed the caller that reaches it through a helper.  (2) **THE FIRST CANDIDATE WAS ALSO OVER-APPLIED, AND NEW BLOCK F IS THAT STATE.** `_gameOver` is `playEnd || getStatus(game)` with no mode term, and `playEnd` is cleared by exactly one function, `fullReset`; nothing clears it on the way into Review or Analyze. So an unscoped picker guard suppressed the picker on the ANALYSIS board too - two taps from any finished game - and a pawn pushed to the last rank did nothing at all, silently, with no error. F drives resign -> Review -> Start review -> the round Analyze button -> a capture-promotion; F1 is RED on gates/.trial/app-nc3-broadguard.js (md5 39b8136aac68) and green once both sites are scoped to `mode==='play'`. Found from two doors independently, by the build re-reading its own diff and by the antagonist driving it.  (3) **D1 IS VACUOUS AND THE CASE PRESENTED IT AS THE LOCALISING MEASUREMENT.** It is not one. In a mated position NO legal move exists, so the ply count cannot advance whatever any guard does - and the particular move D1 plays, e1-e2, is additionally blocked by White's own pawn, which never left e2. D1 is an ARRIVAL CHECK, not a control: it would stay green with every guard in this build deleted. The conclusion it was offered for - that the hole is in the `playEnd` term and not in the `getStatus` term - is still correct, but it is established by READING `humanCanMove` and by C1/C2 going red on endings that are not on the board, not by D1. No input exists where `getStatus` reports terminal and a legal move is available, so that term is not independently testable by this route at all, and the case says so here rather than leaving an assertion that looks like a control and is a tautology.  (4) **TWO COUNTS ARE RESTATED WITH THEIR SCOPE [#411/#412].** The '47 PASS / 0 fail' above was the ARITHMETIC SUM of two subset runs (`CT_B65=A,B` giving 17/0 and `CT_B65=CDE` giving 30/0) presented as though it were a footer, while the #438 control it was compared against was a single process - so the two figures were not the same kind of object. The honest single-process figure for the gate as it now stands, with blocks F and G added, is **58 pass / 0 fail** on the shipping bundle, and the authoritative number is the `65-promotion-after-gameover` section of the full-suite log rather than any figure quoted here. And the paint-only control's '12 pass / 1 FAIL' was a `CT_B65=A` run of 13 assertions, not a run of the gate; published with its command here.  (5) **THE RENDER GUARD HAS NO NEGATIVE CONTROL AND IS NOT CLAIMED TO HAVE ONE.** Every assertion in block A is read at least 250 ms after the flag, long after React has flushed the clearing effect, so a bundle with `!(mode==='play'&&_gameOver)` deleted from the render site would keep this gate green. With `doMove` now guarded the render condition cannot change whether a move lands; it prevents a stale picker being PAINTED for one frame and nothing more. It is defence in depth, this gate cannot prove it, and that is stated rather than dressed up as a controlled assertion.    **ROUND 2 OF THE ANTAGONIST PASS: NO VETO, and three of its notes are folded in here because they make this case auditable rather than merely honest.**  (a) **D1 IS AN ARRIVAL CHECK AND THE `getStatus` TERM IS CONTROLLED ELSEWHERE.** There is no input where `getStatus` reports terminal AND a legal move exists - the two are complements by construction, since `getStatus` returns checkmate/stalemate exactly when the side to move has no legal move. So that term cannot be controlled through MOVE ACCEPTANCE at all, and saying only 'not independently testable' would send the next run down the same dead end. It IS controlled, through a different observable: `gates/regress/63-gameover-chrome-keying.js` block B reaches game over by CHECKMATE, which sets no `playEnd`, and so exercises the `getStatus` half alone through the control row - that gate's own header states the split, and its block B went 10 red on the real shipped #436. Cite 63/B for that term; D1 stays here as the arrival check that the mated state was actually reached, and the move it plays, e1-e2, is additionally blocked by White's own pawn after 1.f3 e5 2.g4 Qh4#.  (b) **THE RENDER GUARD IS PAINT-ONLY DEFENCE IN DEPTH, AND HERE IS EXACTLY WHY THIS GATE CANNOT FAIL ON IT.** With `doMove` guarded, the render condition can no longer change whether a move LANDS; what it stops is a live-looking picker painting over a finished game for the one frame before the clearing effect runs, which matters only because this project has twice filed an enabled-but-inert control as a defect in its own right. Every assertion in block A is read at least 250 ms after the flag, because `waitFlag` polls at 250 ms and that is well past React's effect flush - SO A BUNDLE WITH THE RENDER CONDITION DELETED WOULD KEEP THIS GATE GREEN. Controlling it would need a synchronous read in the same task as the flag detection with the clearing effect kept, which is named here so the next reader knows precisely what would have to change rather than discovering the hole.  (c) **ONLINE CANNOT REACH `playEnd` TODAY, AND THE GUARD WOULD BE A LOCKOUT IF IT EVER COULD.** The committer guard's mode test is true for online as well, so an online game with `playEnd` set would refuse every move with no way to clear it short of leaving. It cannot happen on the current tree, by four independent counts: `resign()` is reachable only from the More sheet, which renders under `mode==='play'&&opponent!=='online'`; the #414 auto-draw excludes online explicitly; and the clock-flag effect needs the LOCAL clock to have run, which is started only in `doMove`'s non-online tail - after the online branch has already returned - and in the computer-only `applyMv`, with `fullReset` initialising it to `run:false`. Recorded as a standing condition rather than a measurement (sign-in and egress are both blocked in this sandbox, so online is UNTRACED): if a later build ever runs the local clock for online games, this guard becomes a lockout and this paragraph is the warning.  (d) **TWO RESIDUALS ARE FILED AS THEIR OWN JOBS RATHER THAN FIXED HERE**, because each would need a rebuild and a fresh 50-minute suite for a window neither the antagonist nor the build could make fire: `playEndRef` lags `playEnd` by one effect flush, and the computer's own auto-move `applyMv` appends a move WITHOUT going through `doMove`, so the committer guard does not cover it - it carries two independent guards of its own and the input that would drive it is the bot flagging mid-search.    **ONE ITEM IS STRUCK FROM THIS CASE'S OWN NOT-COVERED LIST, BECAUSE TWO INDEPENDENT PASSES MEASURED IT [R18].** The list above says the #414 auto-draws by repetition and fifty-move 'set playEnd by the same path as resign and are fixed by construction but unmeasured'. THE THREEFOLD REPETITION DRAW IS NOW MEASURED, twice, from two doors that did not see each other: the auditor drove 8 plies of knight shuffling to `Draw Threefold repetition` and then a further move - the shipped #438 bundle ACCEPTS it, plies 8 to 9, row `4.Ng1 4...Ng8 5.e4`, and the shipping bundle REFUSES it, 8 to 8 - and antagonist B drove the same ending from the shipped-surface door and got the same refusal (8 to 8) with #438 as its own control. So that branch has a real negative control on the real broken build, and 'fixed by construction' is withdrawn in favour of 'measured'. WHAT REMAINS UNMEASURED ON THAT LINE is the FIFTY-MOVE draw and stalemate, which share the same playEnd path and which nobody drove. The assertion is not added to this gate: gate 29 already drives the identical 8 plies, so the cheap home for a standing assertion is there rather than here, and that is a note for whoever takes it rather than a claim that it exists.

TC-PL-037  US-PL-12 clause 3, a finished game goes on STATING its result in the one status line above the board while the player steps back through the moves, and the result card it has already dismissed does not re-open.  *(Added #473, from jobs/play-status-slot-loses-the-result-on-one-back-tap-2026-09-30, found by the uat-internal-challenger on 2026-09-30 at 375x730. THE ID: the job proposes TC-PL-035, which #438 had already used for its landscape case; 037 was checked free against both this document and gates/regress before use. TC-PL-034 is the CONTROL ROW arm of the same clause and stays green throughout - this is the STATUS ROW arm, which #437's sweep did not reach because its author measured the row and the slot was not in view.)*  INPUTS, 6: 2 endings x 3 geometries.
