Re-run after the fix, that mutation (`c10fedd2c2e2`) is **60 PASS / 1 FAIL, E6b RED ALONE**.
   **E7a/E7b/E7c THE COVERAGE LINE SEES STALENESS.** Antagonist B's P0, from the shipped-surface door, and a defect **this build's own mechanism armed**: `_ungr` counted only games with NO entry, so a stale entry read as graded and the single number that suppresses both the coverage line and all three empty-state sentences went to 0 - exactly while the pass rewrites the answer. B measured it at the real 200-game cap with everything stale: the blunder filter answered "26 of 200" and decayed through **27 distinct counts** to "0 of 200" over **23.9 seconds**, `glist-ungraded` null at all 57 samples, ending on the flat "No games match blunders." - the sentence C5b above forbids. On origin/main the same store is a rock-steady "29 of 200". Latent today (GRADE_VER is 1, so the stale set is empty) and armed for the first bump, so it ships FIXED, using the predicate the pass itself uses. THE INPUT IS DETERMINISTIC BY CONSTRUCTION, not by timing: the stale entry sits on a game whose PGN has no moves, so `analyzeGameCounts` returns null, nothing is recorded, and it can never become current however long the pass runs. Reverting the fix (`3d777791cdf5`) is **60 PASS / 1 FAIL, E7b RED ALONE**.
   **THE FOUR CONTROLS NOW REDDEN FOUR DISJOINT SETS** - {E6b}, {E7b}, {E1,E1b,E4,E5}, {E0c,E3,E3b} - which is what establishes that each assertion class is independently falsifiable rather than a restatement of its neighbours: nc-steady `c10fedd2c2e2` 60/1, nc-ungr `3d777791cdf5` 60/1, nc-nostamp `7eab0e05ae69` 57/4, nc-noreview `84d530d1f108` 58/3.
   **AND A SECOND DEFECT IN MY OWN WORK, CAUGHT BY THE GATE GOING RED RATHER THAN BY A REVIEWER.** Rewriting the comment on A's veto, my text replacement consumed the comment block's `*/` terminator, so `GRADE_VER` and `gradeVerOf` fell INSIDE a comment and `gradeCacheUsable` referenced two undefined names. `esbuild` reported the file as PARSING CLEAN - it was syntactically valid, just a longer comment - and the suite caught it as **28 pass / 39 fail**. The tell that pointed at it was arithmetic, not the error text: the bundle came out 9 bytes SMALLER after I had added code. "Parses clean" is not "the code survived compilation"; the check that would have caught it directly is counting the identifiers in esbuild's OUTPUT, which now reads GRADE_VER 3, gradeVerOf 2, gradeCacheUsable 3, and a `/*` vs `*/` balance of 324/324.
   A DEFECT IN THIS CASE'S OWN INSTRUMENT WAS FOUND AND FIXED BEFORE ANY CONTROL WAS RUN, and it is recorded because the green it produced looked exactly like the real one. The first version read its before-picture off the page after the screen had settled, by which time the background pass had already recomputed the stale entries - so it printed `seededGv:1` for an entry the fixture injects at 999, and, far worse, clause (c)'s "unchanged" compared two LATE reads of the same map, which any bundle satisfies if it recomputed that entry before either read. 56 PASS either way. The baseline is now the injected fixture constant, which is the only honest before-picture because it is what was written in. Same family as the trap this project records under "the check and the thing being checked were the same object", wearing a timing costume.
   R36 ADMISSION: deterministic across THREE consecutive runs of the gate as first written against candidate `9b39aa255045` - 56 PASS / 0 FAIL each time, and E1 and E5 returned byte-identical recomputed tallies on all three (`great:2,inacc:1,mist:0,blun:0` at index 1 and `great:2,inacc:2` at index 4), so the recompute is reproducible and not a race the gate happens to win. Runs in one browser session inside the existing gate, adding about 12 seconds. Checks behaviour Kunal would notice: it is the brilliancy filter he asked for telling the truth after the brilliant gate he ruled on actually moves.

| TC-R50 | R36 (suite admission), not a US clause - see the note below | Run `gates/audit/cited-not-run.sh` from the repository root and read its own report. THE PREDICATE IS UNCHANGED and is stated here rather than only in the script: a file under `gates/` that our records cite as the evidence behind a published number AND that is reachable from no suite entry point under the transitive `require()`/`source` closure. The script derives BOTH halves - the reachable set from `gates/gates.sh` (plus `gates/build.sh` and `deploy.py` as tier 2), never from a hand-list, and the cited set from the record documents - which is the whole reason this cell no longer carries a shell one-liner. **WHY THE METHOD MOVED OUT OF THIS CELL, AND IT IS A MEASURED REASON RATHER THAN TIDINESS [R18, R45 shape (1)].** While the method was a one-liner in this cell, every reader re-derived it and no two re-derivations agreed: this one number has been published or proposed at FIVE, ELEVEN, FIFTEEN and SEVENTEEN (then TWENTY-THREE, then THIRTY-EIGHT - see the measured cell) by different derivers across different trees, each correct against the tree and closure rule its deriver used. A number several readers compute several ways is a defect in the method, not in any one reading, and the remedy is the instrument, re-run and re-quoted rather than re-typed. **AND THIS ROW IS ITSELF ONE OF THE TEN SCANNED RECORDS, WHICH MAKES ITS OWN TEXT PART OF WHAT IT MEASURES [R18, same family as "the check and the thing being checked were the same object"].** Rewriting this cell changes the cited set the instrument reads, because the instrument counts filenames named in THIS FILE among its evidence; the figure below is therefore quoted AFTER this row's own text was fixed, re-run until it stopped moving, not before - see the note in the measured cell on the one file this rewrite silently un-cited | **RED, THIRTY-EIGHT CITED-AND-UNREACHABLE, ONE UNACCOUNTED FOR, MEASURED AFTER THIS ROW'S OWN TEXT WAS FIXED [self-referential, see steps cell].** Re-measured 2026-10-14 by process-build-4 (run `process-build-4__1791949999240`, a hand-fired recut) against `origin/main` at `c6c50cc5a327ecf6c0eb21457a6bc9a8e4e432bc`, running the committed script itself, `gates/audit/cited-not-run.sh` md5 `ccab343861b004b39a077692c2066314`, BYTE-IDENTICAL over three consecutive runs AGAINST THE FIXED TEXT, report md5 `7d3aa5de7dfbb94767eac56527f9ff3d` [R36]. That file is deliberately NOT re-named in this cell: doing so would re-cite it and flip the count back to thirty-nine, an oscillation rather than a fix, which is the real lesson this withdrawal carries - a measurement that names its own evidence by filename inside the very document it scans is not at a fixed point until a re-run after the edit agrees with the run before it, and this one did not agree the first time it was tried. There is no gate and there cannot be one: the subject is the tracker, and no script in this repository can read it. **ARM A, THE SCHEMA.** Query `jobs` with the single where-clause `priority >= ""`. The store orders every number before every string, so that one clause returns EXACTLY the documents whose `priority` is string-typed - no type predicate and no enumeration of spellings. **ARM B, THE CARRY.** Take the newest `runledger` row whose lane is build and whose status is done, read the open P0 and open P1 figures it published, and re-derive both at that row's own finishedAt by the four-reading union this job's reportAt432 and reportAt438 fields define: the title's leading P-n prefix, a numeric `priority` equal to n, a string `priority` equal to 'P-n', and a `severity` field beginning P-n. | **ARM A PASSES AT 0 DOCUMENTS RETURNED. ARM B PASSES when each published figure equals its re-derived one.** INPUT COUNT, counted and not estimated: arm A runs over EVERY document in `jobs`, 781 of them at the reading below, enumerated in four pages of 200 and cross-checked document by document; arm B over the 2 published figures of 1 build row. **ARM A IS RED AT TWELVE AND THE QUERY WAS VERIFIED AGAINST AN INDEPENDENT ENUMERATION, which matters more than the twelve:** the one-clause query returned 12 documents, and a separate full walk of all 781 classified them independently as 760 int, 12 str, 9 with NO `priority` KEY AT ALL - the same 12, by two methods that share no code. **THE NINE ARE A THIRD CLASS THIS JOB'S OWN `scope` FIELD DOES NOT NAME.** It describes two incompatible types; absence is a third, it is invisible to a type query and to every priority-ordered sort alike, and eight of the nine are `bench-` jobs that read `ready`. A register with 9 unsortable rows cannot be counted consistently even once both types are migrated, so the schema half is not finished by picking a type. | by hand, no gate - the two queries above, quoted so a stranger can re-run them rather than trust them | **RED AT TWELVE on arm A**, measured 2026-10-07T22:0xZ by process-build lane 4 against the live collection, and published as a verdict-with-its-command rather than as a figure pointing at a log that cannot exist - the same honest shape TC-R50's own last cell argues for. **ARM B WAS NOT RUN THIS RUN and is NOT claimed green:** it needs the newest build row plus a four-reading union over all 781 documents, the fourth reading of which (`severity`) was measured on 2026-10-04 to be ABSENT from 72 per cent of ready jobs, so the arm needs its own run and its own scope note. **WITHDRAWN IN THE CELL THAT WOULD HAVE CARRIED IT:** this row's first draft was going to publish the 2026-10-04 census figures - 591 int against 25 string, 15 of them 'P-n' - as its measurement. Both halves are now wrong (12, not 25; 781 documents, not 616) and the register has grown by 165 documents since, so the stale pair is recorded here as superseded instead of being quoted as current. |

**AND THEN IT WAS PUBLISHED AS "FIVE" AND THAT WAS ALSO FALSE, BY TEN. SECOND WITHDRAWAL ON THE SAME ROW,
RECORDED IN THE SAME PLACE AS THE FIRST [R18].** 2026-10-06, `beb4ed4`, `process-build-4__1791280138915`,
working step (3) of `jobs/a-test-in-the-repo-that-no-suite-runs-2026-09-28`'s own `whatIsLeft`. The first
withdrawal above fixed an ABSENCE claim - zero where the answer was not zero. This one fixes something
narrower and more durable: **the published command was less recursive than the published predicate.** The steps
cell says "every `.js` under `gates/` and `gates/pending/`"; the measured cell's command was
`for f in $(ls gates/*.js)`, which lists ONE directory level. Ten of the fifteen true members live in
`gates/audit/` and `gates/drive/`, so the figure could not have been right at any point since the row was
written, and re-reading the row would never have shown it - only re-running a DIFFERENT command does. That is
the general lesson and it is worth more than the number: **when a cell carries both a predicate and a command,
the two can disagree, and the number will always be the command's.** Check them against each other, not the
number against your memory of it.

THE SECOND ARM, AND IT MOVED THE COUNT THE OTHER WAY. The old method had no `require()` closure, so it counted
`gates/engine-extract.js` and `gates/lib.js` as unreachable when both are `require`d from gates that run on
every full suite - `engine-extract.js` from `gates/regress/68-brilliant-sac-empty-square.js`. Without the
closure arm the answer is 17; with it, 15. A reachability check on a Node harness that does not follow
`require` is measuring file names, not execution.

WHAT THIS CORRECTION DOES NOT DO, said plainly rather than left for the next reader to discover.
**Not one of the fifteen has been admitted to a suite, and this row is a count, not a fix.** Admission needs
`gates/gates.sh`, and `gates/gates.sh`'s log-section count is pinned by the roster arm of `gates/verify-log.sh` - the one that refuses any full log whose count of `^=== ` lines differs from the roster in its "gates ran (N)" line - **cited by symbol and not by line number on purpose: the first draft of this paragraph wrote a bare line-range cite into it and `gates/verify-log.sh --citations` arm (4) went RED on it, 49 line citations against a committed ceiling of 48, before the change left this run.** That ceiling is the mechanism working: a line number in a register rots the moment the file it points into is edited, which is exactly what this row has now been corrected for twice. So an
extra `=== ` header refuses every green log from that point on - two files, one of which another lane was
holding on 2026-10-06, and R44 forbids starting a job while holding half of what it needs. Step (2) of that
job's `whatIsLeft` therefore remains open and unclaimed by this run. **Nor is "unreachable" the same as
"should be admitted":** five of the fifteen are the `gates/audit/` screen auditors and five are the
`gates/drive/` drivers, which are plausibly tools rather than gates, and nothing in this run read them to
decide. The honest state is a correct count of a set whose members have not been triaged one by one.

ON THE ID `TC-R22`, BECAUSE A TRACKER DOCUMENT ASKS FOR IT AND THIS FILE ALREADY ANSWERS IT. That job's
`scopeReducedTo` says "Case TC-R22 in `claude/stories/TEST-CASES.md` ... No TC-R22 row exists on main", and it
is right that none exists and wrong that one should be written: this very case WAS `TC-R22`, was renumbered to
`TC-R50` before #477's push, and `TC-R22` is live and differently scoped in
`claude/stories/SUITE-AUDIT-2026-09-14.md` - see the note on the id below, which predates the request. Writing
the row the tracker asks for would recreate by hand exactly the collision that note records avoiding. **No
`TC-R22` row has been added and none should be; the case the tracker is asking after is this one.**

**THIS ROW WAS PUBLISHED AS "ZERO" AND THAT WAS FALSE. RECORDED RATHER THAN QUIETLY FIXED [R18].** #477 wrote
this case to close `jobs/a-test-in-the-repo-that-no-suite-runs-2026-09-28`, whose subject is

antagonists measured the real answer independently and got four and five. It is an ABSENCE CLAIM, in a cell
whose own closing paragraph is titled "what this case cannot see, said plainly because an absence claim is
the hardest kind", and the thing it could not see was the thing it asserted. CLAUDE.md: *"'This does not
exist' must list the screens and states actually checked."* The repair is the one #405 names: publish the
command with the count, and let the case be RED when it is red.

**AND "FIVE" IS NOW WITHDRAWN TOO, IN THE SAME CELL THAT CARRIED IT, 2026-10-07 [R18].** The paragraph above
records that this row was once published as ZERO. It was then published as FIVE for three days, and FIVE is
also wrong - not because the 2026-10-04 derivation was careless, but because it was taken at `2e5f30b` from a
one-liner that read `gates/*.js` only (one directory, no `gates/audit/`, no `gates/drive/`, no
`gates/pending/`) and with no `require()` closure. Against `origin/main` at `109d897` the committed instrument
reads **23**. The full history of this one figure, kept because the shape of the error is worth more than the
number: ZERO (#477, the count true of the one file already fixed), FIVE (hand-read at `2e5f30b`, published
here), SEVENTEEN (process-build lane 3, 2026-10-04, base `842df1b`, no closure), FIFTEEN (process-build lane
4, 2026-10-06, base `beb4ed4`, with closure - applied clean at #482 and then VETOED by both of that build's
antagonists, independently and from different doors), ELEVEN (#482 antagonist B, re-running lane 4's own
published command and finding four of its ten new members `require()`d by gates the suite runs), and now
TWENTY-THREE. **NOT ONE OF THE SIX IS WITHDRAWN AS A MISTAKE OF ARITHMETIC, and that is the finding.** Each
is what the predicate gives at the tree and closure rule its deriver used, and `gates/audit/cited-not-run.sh`
itself records that the set went 5 to 17 to 23 as the repository grew. What was wrong was publishing the
figure with its method as prose in a table cell, where the method could not be re-run and could not be
pinned to a tree. That is R45 shape (1) - one named measure, several values - and it cost two antagonist
passes and one vetoed payload before anybody said so. **THE REPAIR IS THE INSTRUMENT, NOT A SIXTH NUMBER.**
This row now cites a committed, deterministic script and publishes what it prints, with the sha it was run
at; the next reader re-runs one command instead of reconstructing a one-liner. Anyone tempted to correct this
figure again should check first whether the instrument disagrees with the row, because if it does the row is
simply stale against a moved tree and the fix is to re-run it and quote the new sha - which is the whole
point of moving the method out of the cell.

**ON THE LAST CELL, SAID OUT LOUD SO IT IS NOT A DODGE.** `verify-log.sh --citations` check (2) requires any
row whose LAST cell carries a `PASS`/`FAIL` token to have its id occur in the log that cell's section cites.
This row has no gate and therefore no log, so its result is published as "ACCOUNTED-GREEN AT TWENTY-THREE"
with the committed script and the sha that produce it rather than as a FAIL figure that would point at a log which cannot exist. That is the honest
shape for a case with no runner, and it is stated here rather than left as a convenient silence - a reader
who wants the verdict has it in the same cell.

**NOTE ON THE ID, AND IT IS THE SAME TWO-SPACE COLLISION `USER-STORIES.md` ALREADY RECORDS FOR US-R16.** This
case was first written as TC-R22 and renumbered to **TC-R50** before the push. `TC-R22` is NOT free: it is a

board", recorded **FAIL at #373**, carrying its own replacement spec and referenced six times in that file.
A duplicate would have made no sentence of the form "TC-R22 passed" resolvable, with one of the two on record
as failing. The id was checked against `TEST-CASES.md` only, which is the mistake: **the id space spans every
file under `claude/stories/`, including that frozen 2026-09-14 audit.** `TC-R49` is also taken - reserved by
`jobs/a-count-from-the-background-estimate-map-is-printed-as-the-players-own-reviewed-games-2026-10-04` - so
the next free id after this row **is NOT TC-R51 - that claim is WITHDRAWN here [R18]**. Re-measured
2026-10-07 across all 84 refs under `refs/remotes/origin`, every id through TC-R54 is in use on some ref and
TC-R51 is also reserved by an open job; of the gaps, 32 is now TAKEN BY THE ROW ABOVE and 33, 34, 38 and 40
are free, while 36 and 39 are reserved by open jobs. The next free id above the block is TC-R55, itself
spoken for by the renumbering note on that same open job, so TC-R56 is the first unspoken-for id above it.
Check `git grep -o -E 'TC-R[0-9]+' $(git for-each-ref --format='%(refname)' refs/remotes/origin)` over
`claude` and `gates`, AND the tracker's open jobs - not one file, and not one ref.

**WHY THIS CASE HAS NO STORY CLAUSE, AND WHY THAT IS CORRECT [R08].** No `USER-STORIES.md` clause governs the
harness, and none should - a user story is about what a player can do. The governing rule is R36, suite
admission: a test joins the permanent suite only if it is deterministic across three runs, fast enough to run
on every build, and checks behaviour Kunal would notice. `gates/unit-drill-why.js` met all three - it is a
pure unit test with no browser - so R36 said it should be admitted rather than left beside the suite, and for
weeks it was neither admitted nor rejected. Recorded here rather than inventing a US id.

**THE UNIT LAYER CONTRIBUTES EXACTLY 40 PASS LINES TO THE FOOTER, AND THAT IS A ONE-TIME STEP RATHER THAN A
REGRESSION — BUT IT IS NOT THE WHOLE OF ANY FOOTER'S RISE, AND THE FIRST DRAFT OF THIS PARAGRAPH SAID IT WAS
[R18].** Measured over the committed gatelogs: `grep -c '^PASS unit-drill-why'` is **0** in `474-all.log`
and **40** in `475-all.log`, `476-all.log` and `477-all.log`, so #475 is the unit layer's first full run and
40 is its exact contribution. **The footer that run went 3784 to 4059, a rise of 275**, because #475 also
added a 55th section (gate 72). So the honest statement is "the unit layer accounts for 40 of it", not "the
footer rose by 40" — a later reader comparing two footers needs the attribution, not the total. The 40 are
checks that were running and being cited and counting for nothing.

**WHAT THIS CASE CANNOT SEE, said plainly because an absence claim is the hardest kind [R18].** It reads
filenames, not behaviour. A gate that `gates.sh` reaches, runs, and that asserts nothing is invisible to it -
that is the empty-gate shape, and `gates.sh` covers it separately by treating a section which exits clean
having asserted nothing as red. A gate that is MISSING from the directory altogether is also invisible to it,
and that is `gates/gate-manifest.tsv`'s job. Three different holes, three different instruments, and this one
is only the third. It also cannot see a file cited as evidence somewhere other than `RUN-LOG.md` and the last
five run reports - a flag, a job, a tracker document - so FIVE is a floor and the scope is named rather than
implied.

### TC-R51 (US-R38) — the promotion picker offers the promoting side's own pieces

**GATE** `gates/regress/65-promotion-after-gameover.js`, blocks **J** and **JS**. `CT_APP=<bundle> CT_B65=J,JS node gates/regress/65-promotion-after-gameover.js`

**INPUTS — 2 geometries x 2 sides = 4 driven promotions.** `kunal730` (375x730) and `se` (320x568), portrait. Per geometry: one BLACK promotion, reached in Pass & Play by `1.d4 e5 2.c3 exd4 3.h3 dxc3 4.h4 cxb2 5.h5` then `b2xa1`, which is the mirror of block E's white line with White's h-pawn supplying the waiting moves; and one WHITE promotion in its own browser, reached by block E's own line `1.e4 d5 2.exd5 c6 3.dxc6 a6 4.cxb7 a5` then `b7-a8`.

**PASS CONDITION, and the instrument is the board rather than a threshold.** A piece in this app is an `<img>` carrying its own sprite, so "which piece is the picker showing" has an exact answer with no pixel scan and no luminance threshold: read the sprite the BOARD uses for a given piece and colour off the start position with `pieceAt`, then compare. For the black promotion — (J1) all four picker buttons contain an `<img>`; (J2) those four srcs equal, in order, the board's own black queen, rook, bishop and knight read off d8, a8, c8 and b8; (J3) no button carries a text glyph. For the white promotion — (J4) the four srcs equal the board's own white sprites read off d1, a1, c1 and b1. Instruments asserted first and separately: (J0c) the board's black and white queen sprites are both readable AND DIFFERENT, without which J2 is unfalsifiable; (J0a) the move row shows `cxb2`, so the position really was reached; (J0b) the picker is open with four enabled 52x52 buttons; (J4a) the white sprites are readable and differ from the black queen; (J5/J4c) zero console errors.

**BLOCK JS — THE SYMBOL PIECE SET, 8 assertions at 375x730.** `ct_pieceSet='symbol'` seeded before load, so `_Piece` takes its `useFallback` branch and draws text glyphs. Asserts the set is really in effect (the board draws 0 images), that the picker opened 4/4 with the four BLACK glyphs, and that the glyph's **contrast against its own composited button face is at least 3:1** — compositing every ancestor layer in order rather than stopping at the first opaque one, which is the #432 instrument error that put a threshold between two readings. JS2 repeats it for a WHITE promotion so black cannot be fixed at white's expense. One geometry only, deliberately: a contrast ratio has no viewport term, so a second geometry would buy repetition rather than coverage.

**WHAT THE BROKEN BUILD DOES DIFFERENTLY.** On control bundle `4c7585a3697f` — built from the #496 tree by reverting exactly the two edited regions of `chess.jsx` and nothing else — the four buttons carry NO `<img>` at all and their text reads `♛♜♝♞` for Black and `♕♖♗♘` for White, every one of them painted `#fff`. Measured on the shipped bundle `01387f706cea`: block J **24 pass / 0 fail**, block JS **8 pass / 0 fail**.

**THREE CONTROLS, because the three things this build changed fail in three different places and no single bundle exercises all of them.** Each is this tree with one thing reverted and nothing else:

| control | what is reverted | result |
|---|---|---|
| A — `4c7585a3697f` | the whole fix (main's `chess.jsx`) | block J **16 / 8** — J1, J2, J3, J4 at both geometries |
| B — `e25df6971f4b` | only the button's face, back to the accent tint | block JS **7 / 1** — JS1 alone, black at **1.33:1**, while white reads 13.06:1 and JS2 stays green |
| C — `cba731dce809` | only the `aria-label` (the `48c5ffa` tree) | block J **22 / 2** — J1b alone, `names:["","","",""]` |

Every instrument stays green on all three, so in each case the reds are the assertions about the thing under test and nothing else. **Note what control A does NOT redden: J1b passes on it, and correctly** — the old build's text glyph *was* the button's accessible name, so a control that predates the image cannot redden an assertion about a name it still had. That is why control C exists; a reader checking only control A would conclude J1b is unfalsifiable.

**THE ASSERTION THIS CASE DELIBERATELY DOES NOT MAKE, recorded because it is the one a reader will reach for.** "The picker's queen is NOT the white queen sprite" states the defect in the job's own words and is VACUOUS on the broken bundle: there is no `<img>` there, so `null !== whiteQueen` is true and the assertion passes loudest on the one bundle where the screen does exactly what the job complains about. That is #432's "a check that reads only what the fix added cannot see the defect the fix removes". The colour claim is therefore carried by the POSITIVE identity in J2, with J0c proving the two sets are distinguishable first, and the "draws text, not a piece" half is J3 — a separate `L.say` and not a conjunct, per the non-empty-denominator rule.

**NOT CHECKED, and these are holes rather than passes.** The SKIN half of US-R38 clause (2) is still proved by MECHANISM only for the four IMAGE sets — J2 shows the picker reads the same sprite source the board reads, which is how a skin reaches it, but nothing switches between `classic`, `merida`, `chessnut` and `spatial` and re-reads the picker, so a build that hard-coded `PIECE_IMG` would pass every assertion here and still ignore a skin. Block JS closes only the FIFTH set, `symbol`. **ELEVEN OF THE TWELVE THEMES ARE UNMEASURED IN THE BROWSER:** `TH.light`/`TH.dark` differ per theme and JS drives only whatever a fresh store defaults to; the twelve-theme table in US-R38 is computed from `THEMES` (chess.jsx:1571) by arithmetic, not driven, and it names Candy's white cell at 2.86:1 as the one below the floor. Landscape is not visited: block H covers the picker's layout at 730x375 and says nothing about which side's pieces it draws, nor about contrast. No assertion reads the knight or bishop's identity INDEPENDENTLY of the board's own read of that square, so a build corrupting both the picker and the board identically would pass — note this is *not* the same as a transposition, which J2's ordered compare does catch.

**WITHDRAWN FROM THIS LIST [R18].** An earlier draft of the gate's own NOT-CHECKED list said "a transposition inside the set that preserved the four members would pass". False — J2 is `JSON.stringify(imgs.srcs)===JSON.stringify([bq,br,bb,bn])`, an ordered compare. Antagonist B caught the gate understating its own coverage and disagreeing with this case, which had it right.

**NOTE ON THE ID [#496].** TC-R51 is the id this register's own TC-R50 note names as next free, checked with `grep -rn 'TC-R51' claude/stories/` (one hit, that sentence) and `grep -r 'TC-R51' .` over the repository. TC-R58 was deliberately NOT taken: it is specified-but-unadmitted on `jobs/rank-up-verdict-line-is-cut-mid-word-2026-09-28`, and a reserved id is invisible to grep because it lives on the tracker and not in the tree.

### TC-R60 (US-R50) — a modal dialog's own controls own their own centres

**GATE** `gates/regress/74-lesson-card-hit-area.js`. `CT_APP=<bundle> node gates/regress/74-lesson-card-hit-area.js`, and `CT_74_GEOS=se,short375` to restrict the geometry list.

**INPUTS — 6 named lessons x 3 geometries = 18 driven intro cards, plus one demo state.** *(This line read "4 named lessons x 3 geometries = 12" until #498 and is WITHDRAWN [R18]: correction 7 below added Petroff (Russian) Defense and Gambits row 10 in the same build that wrote this line, and the INPUTS header was not updated with them. The lesson list in the gate is the authority — it is a named array — and it holds six.)* Geometries `se` (320x568), `short375` (375x568) and `kunal730` (375x730), portrait. Lessons: Openings row 0 (Italian Game), row 4 (Four Knights Game), row 20 (Queen's Gambit) and Gambits row 0 (King's Gambit). **They are driven by index and asserted by NAME** (B0b): if the lesson list is reordered the gate goes red rather than silently measuring another card. **Four and not one, because the defect is per-lesson** — the card is vertically centred so the CTA's y is set by the lesson's own content, and the Italian Game is *clean on the broken bundle*, so a gate that opened only the first lesson would be a coin flip. The four are chosen to include the clean case and the three worst measured.

**REACHING THE STATE IS ITSELF AN ASSERTION (A1), and this is the part that did not exist before.** The intro card auto-dismisses 4000ms after it opens unless it is touched (`chess.jsx`, search `introHoldRef`). This gate opens the lesson, clicks the card's **PANEL** to hold it, waits **4600ms**, and asserts the card is STILL UP before measuring anything. **It does not use `D.states['intro']`**: that driver state's hold-tap walks up from the CTA to the first fixed-or-absolute ancestor and clicks *that* rect, which is the OVERLAY, whose own `onClick` is `setIntroCard(false)` — so the tap meant to hold the card dismisses it. Measured: after `D.states['intro']` the CTA is ABSENT at all five of 320x568, 375x568, 375x679, 375x730 and 390x844. That is why no existing gate could assert anything about this state, and A1 is the permanent control on it.

**PASS CONDITION, and the scoping is the whole difficulty.** The gate computes the **LIVE MODAL LAYER** mechanically: it collects the full-viewport `position:fixed` overlays on screen, takes the one with the highest z-index as the live modal, and asserts over **its own descendants and nothing else**. For each such control, `document.elementFromPoint` at the control's own centre, and at 25% and 75% of its height, must return that control or a descendant of it.

  - **B1** every control in the live modal layer owns its centre, 25% and 75% point. 4 or 5 controls per card.
  - **C1** THE CONSEQUENCE, BY A REAL TAP: a real mouse click at the CTA's own centre dismisses the card AND leaves the player in the lesson. Three separate readings (`inLessonBefore`, `cardUpAfter`, `inLessonAfter`), because on the broken bundle the tap went to the footer and **the card stayed up**, and in one case the lesson CLOSED — two different wrong outcomes that one boolean could not tell apart.
  - **A2 / A2b** a fixed bottom bar IS on screen and one of them is the lesson footer at z-index 471. Without this the whole gate is vacuous and would pass on a build that deleted the footer.
  - **A3** THE MECHANISM, asserted in the bundle under test and not only its symptom: the card overlay's own z-index is ABOVE the footer's. It read 120 against 471 on #496.
  - **B2** THE DENOMINATOR, as its own `L.say` and never a conjunct: all 4 named cards were actually measured. `every()` over an empty set is true, so an assertion whose input set can empty reports PASS loudest exactly when the state has disappeared.
  - **E0/E1/E2** THE OTHER DIRECTION: in the plain demo state NO modal is up, so the live layer is the document, the fixed bars are in scope, and all 9 controls — the footer's five included — own their centres. This is what stops the fix being "raise the overlay until nothing collides": a bar that could no longer be used in the ordinary demo state would redden here.

**THE ANTI-VACUITY GUARD IS SPLIT IN TWO, AND THE SPLIT IS THE POINT.** B1 can only fail at a geometry where some card's CTA actually reaches the bar's band. **At 375x730 — Kunal's own phone — none of the four does** (the tallest ends at 627.22 against a footer top of 669), so B1's green there is a POSITIVE CONTROL and says nothing about the defect. Per geometry the gate therefore emits an explicit **NOTE** rather than a PASS (`74 B3 [geo] NOT EXERCISED HERE`), which is the empty-denominator rule's own wording: *make it a red, or an explicit note that says it did not run, and never a PASS.* A per-geometry red would be a permanent red on a correct tree, which is how a guard gets switched off. The hard assertion is **B3G**, once per run: at least one geometry in the list must exercise the defect, or B1 and C1 are decoration everywhere. It is global so that adding a tall-screen geometry can never silently disarm the gate.

**WHAT THE BROKEN BUILD DOES DIFFERENTLY.** The control is `origin/main`'s OWN bundle — free, and the actual broken build. `CT_APP=gates/.trial/mainbundle.js CT_74_GEOS=se,short375 node gates/regress/74-lesson-card-hit-area.js` over bundle **01387f706cea** gives **29 pass / 15 fail**:

| geometry | what reddens |
|---|---|
| `se` | A3 (mechanism, z 120 vs 471); B1 + C1 on Four Knights, Queen's Gambit and King's Gambit; B4 lost-count 3 |
| `short375` | A3; B1 on all three; C1 on Queen's Gambit and King's Gambit; B4 lost-count 3 |

**AND THE ONE READING WORTH MORE THAN THE COUNT:** at `short375` on King's Gambit the control records `cardUpAfter:false, inLessonAfter:FALSE` — the tap at the CTA's own centre hit `✕ Close lesson` and **the lesson closed**. That is the job's headline claim, which had been asserted from a different lesson and a 44px footer reading, reproduced here on main's own bundle with the geometry and lesson named. On the fixed bundle **1147cf982542** every geometry reads 0 fail.

**WHAT PASSES ON THE BROKEN BUNDLE, checked deliberately rather than assumed.** B3 stays GREEN at `se` and `short375` on the control (3 of 4 cards reach the bar) — which is correct and is what makes B1 able to fail; if B3 had reddened too, the gate would have been reporting "I could not test this" instead of "this is broken". A1, A2, A2b, B0b, B2 and the whole E block are green on both bundles, because they are instruments and not the thing under test. That is the shape a sound control has: the reds are the assertions about the defect and nothing else.

**NOT CHECKED, and these are holes rather than passes.**
  - **LANDSCAPE.** Not visited at any geometry. The footer is `left:0;right:0;bottom:0` so it exists in landscape too, and a 320-tall landscape viewport is the most likely place for this class to be worse than anything measured here.
  - **LARGER ACCESSIBILITY FONTS.** The suite varies seven geometries and exactly one font. Every card's height is text-driven, so a larger font is the obvious route from a clean lesson to an affected one, and nothing here covers it.
  - **166 OF THE 170 LESSON CARDS.** The population was measured once, on the broken bundle, by a throwaway probe; the GATE drives four. So the gate holds the class at four points and the 46/170 figure is a one-time measurement, not a standing assertion. If a lesson's text grows, nothing re-counts.
  - **THE PRACTICE PHASE AND THE ⋯ SHEET** are not asserted over by this gate. The sheet is bottom-anchored and scrollable, so its controls are reachable by scrolling and a naive probe over it produces the #393 false positives; a correct assertion for it needs the reachability treatment (`40-reachability.js`) and not this one.
  - **THE PUZZLE CELEBRATION.** The third member of the class could not be reached by any route this run tried, so it is unmeasured. A fixture that opens it is what this needs.
  - **A REAL DEVICE.** Every number is headless Chromium at dpr 1, and the fonts in this container resolve to DejaVu Sans, so every text-derived height here is container-dependent [#480].

**THE ASSERTION THIS CASE DELIBERATELY DOES NOT MAKE.** "No control is ever covered by another element" — false by construction, since a modal is supposed to cover what is behind it, and measured: it returns 12 of 42 lesson states red on a CORRECT build, eleven of them the lesson's own buttons legitimately behind a modal. #393 tried three versions of that assertion and shipped none, and this gate's live-modal-layer scoping is the answer to it: a control is excused only when a **strictly higher full-viewport fixed overlay exists that does not contain it**, which is pinned to the mechanism rather than to a count of exceptions.

**NOTE ON THE ID.** TC-R60 is one above the highest case id known anywhere (TC-R59), taken deliberately rather than filling a gap. This case was first written as **TC-R52**, which `grep -r 'TC-R52' .` cleared and which is in fact RESERVED by `jobs/bench-lesson-complete-has-nowhere-to-go-2026-09-28` with a six-input spec already written against it — the exact false clear TC-R51's own note predicts. Sixteen TC ids are reserved on the tracker and absent from this file. The near miss and the method that caught it are recorded on US-R50.

**CORRECTIONS TO TC-R60, MADE BEFORE THE PUSH ON TWO UPHELD BLIND-ANTAGONIST VETOES [R18, #497].** Both antagonists vetoed, independently, from the diff door and the shipped-surface door, and they converged on eight findings. Every number below is withdrawn and replaced in the document that carried it rather than corrected elsewhere.

1. **"46 of 170" IS NOT THE NUMBER A PLAYER FEELS, AND THE SUMMARY LINE OF THE SWEEP MISLABELS IT.** 46 is the count of cards with **at least one of three** sample points off the CTA. Counted again, by hand, over the same log: on **19** of those 46 the CENTRE still hit the CTA and only the 25% or 75% edge strayed. **So the centre-tap-lost figure is 27 of 170, not 46**, and the 46 covers a fat-finger defect as well as an unusable one. The sweep log's own final line reads "LESSONS WHOSE CTA CENTRE IS NOT ITS OWN: 46 of 170", which is false by its own rows. Both antagonists found this; I re-derived 19/27 myself rather than taking it.

2. **"A TAP CLOSES THE LESSON" IS ONE MEASURED OBSERVATION, AT 375x568, AND ZERO AT 320x568.** Counted over all 46 affected cards at 320x568: the centre hits the footer BAR on 11, "Forward a move" on 11, a chevron `svg` on 5 — and **`✕ Close lesson` on NONE**. The lesson-closing outcome is measured twice and both are at **375x568**: King's Gambit (gate 74's control, `inLessonAfter:false`) and Petroff, where all three sample points hit `✕`. The job that drove this build states the symptom at 320x568; that is not where it reproduces, and the clause and the gate now say 375x568.

3. **"Tactics 0/0" WAS AN EMPTY DENOMINATOR PRESENTED AS A CLEAN GROUP.** The Tactics tile routes to a trainer, not a lesson list, so it has no rows and **cannot show an intro card at all**. Reporting it beside "Openings 8/97" reads as a fourth group measured and found clean. It is reported and not credited now. It does not move 46/170, which totals 97+53+20.

4. **"AT 375x730 NO CARD REACHES THE FOOTER" RESTED ON 4 CARDS OF 170 — 2.4%.** There is no 375x730 sweep; the four named cards end at most 627.22 against a footer top of 669, a 41.78px margin, while the 320x568 population spans 370.25..540.28, a 170px spread. **The defensible claim is "not reproduced on the cards sampled"**, and that matters because gate 74's B3 declares that geometry a positive control on the strength of those same four. The gate's note now says so.

5. **THE `panel=` COLUMN IN THE PUBLISHED SWEEP LOG IS WRONG IN 170 OF 170 ROWS, AND IT IS THE COLUMN THE WHOLE MECHANISM ARGUMENT RESTS ON.** Every row reads `panel=<CTA y>+44`, i.e. the probe reported the CTA's own rect and labelled it the panel: its walk kept the child it started from instead of the child of the fixed overlay. The panel is hundreds of px tall, never 44. The verdict column is unaffected and the 46/170 stands, but the diagnostic that was supposed to SHOW "the CTA's y is a function of the card's height" showed nothing. Antagonist B checked all 170 rows. **That log should not be cited for the panel figure; the panel measurements in this case come from a second probe that walks it correctly, and they are the -33.27..601.28 figures below.**

6. **THE EVIDENCE PUBLISHED FOR THE FIXED BUNDLE DID NOT ATTACH TO WHAT WAS BEING SHIPPED, THREE WAYS AT ONCE, AND BOTH ANTAGONISTS FOUND IT.** (a) Its footer read `GATE: 62 pass, 1 fail` while its body held 42 `^PASS` and 1 `^FAIL` — the 320x568 block, the only geometry the defect is about, had been lost from the file when a foreground command was moved to the background. That is the #405 thin-log shape. (b) That run was of a **pre-correction gate**: its B3 emitted a per-geometry FAIL and no B3G line, wording that does not exist in the committed file. (c) The bundle on disk had been **rebuilt** after the run, so no log named it. All three are why the suite was stopped and re-run from the top over a frozen tree, and why the figures in this case come from the final run and not that one.

7. **THE SET NOW DRIVES SIX LESSONS, NOT FOUR, AND THE RESIDUAL IS ASSERTED RATHER THAN EXCLUDED.** The first draft drove Openings 0/4/20 and Gambits 0 while the gate header and the manifest row both claimed the set "includes the worst" — and the worst card, **Openings row 45, Petroff (Russian) Defense**, was the one card left out, named in the same sentence that excluded it. Added, with **Gambits row 10** as a second Gambits sample because 37 of 53 Gambits cards are affected and one row was not a sample of that.

   **AND PETROFF IS NOT FIXED BY THIS BUILD.** Measured on both bundles at 320x568, inside the auto-dismiss window: panel **-33.27..601.28** (height 634.55) in a 568 viewport, CTA **540.28..584.28** so 16.28px is below the screen, close ✕ **-23.27..6.73** so 6.73px of 30 is on it. Z-index is paint, not layout, so none of those moves. What the fix does change for it is real and is the hit test: **own 25/50/75 goes from false/false/false on main to true/true/(outside the viewport)**. Two different faults, now asserted separately — **B1** over the sample points that are ON the screen, **B1b** over whether a control is on the screen at all, with a per-card ceiling of 2 (Petroff's CTA and its ✕) and **B1bG** listing every instance each run so a third cannot appear in silence. The overflow is filed as `jobs/the-lesson-intro-card-can-be-taller-than-the-viewport-and-nothing-scrolls-it-2026-10-08`.

8. **THE HEADLINE ASSERTION HAD NO DENOMINATOR OF ITS OWN, AND THE GATE'S HEADER CLAIMED IT DID.** B1 is `bad.length===0` over the live modal layer's controls; an EMPTY set makes that its loudest PASS over nothing. The header said B2 covered it — B2 counts **cards**, a different denominator. **B1a** now asserts `controls > 0` in its own `L.say`, beside B1, exactly as the E block already did. And the route was live, not theoretical: `layer()` picked the top overlay by z-index alone, so the app's `data-ct="layout-grid"` overlay (`position:fixed inset:0 zIndex:9990 pointerEvents:'none'`) would have won it and contributed zero buttons. `layer()` now excludes `pointerEvents:'none'` overlays, because an overlay that cannot take a tap is not a modal layer however high its z-index.

9. **A3 WAS NOT PINNED TO THE CARD AND ITS LABEL PRINTED A FALSEHOOD WHEN IT FAILED.** It asserted `modalZ > footZ` for whatever overlay won the z pick, saying nothing about the intro card; it now also requires the z to be exactly **472** and **A3b** asserts separately that the overlay measured is the one containing the CTA. And its message was phrased as the desired state with actuals substituted, so every red printed "(120) is ABOVE the footer's (471)" — the opposite of its own reading. It states the measurement now and computes the verdict word.

10. **A2 AND A2b WERE DESCRIBED AS THE NON-VACUITY GUARD AND CANNOT FAIL.** `lessonFocus` is defined as exactly the intro card's own first two conjuncts and the footer renders on `lessonFocus`, so the footer is present whenever the card is, by construction. They are labelled DIAGNOSTIC now; the guard is B3/B3G.

11. **THE B3 NOTE CLAIMED CLEANLINESS AT 375x679, A GEOMETRY THE GATE DOES NOT RUN.** Struck.

12. **THE HOLD-TAP IS CLAMPED INTO THE VIEWPORT.** Petroff's panel top is -33.27, so an unclamped hold at `panelTop+10` lands off the screen, the hold never registers, the 4000ms auto-dismiss fires and the card is gone: measured as `CARD GONE - hold failed` on **both** bundles. The clamp is why Petroff can be asserted over at all, and `openHeld` reports when it clamped.

### TC-R58 (US-R47) — the news survives the box, at the input that actually exposes it

**ADMITTED AT #502** for `jobs/rank-up-verdict-line-is-cut-mid-word-2026-09-28` (ship-list id 8). The case was specified in full on that job on 2026-09-28 and never admitted here; #496 deliberately left the id free because it was reserved. This is that admission, **with the input count and the input itself replaced by measured ones** — the job says "THE DENOMINATOR IS NOT COUNTED [R07] and that count IS the class sweep this job still owes", and "whoever runs the sweep replaces the input count here."

**THE SWEEP THAT SETS THE DENOMINATOR [R07], WITH ITS FIRST ANSWER WITHDRAWN.** The class is *a capped box carrying a state sentence concatenated with an event announcement*. My first count was **110 ternaries with an empty-string false arm, of which 25 appending occurrences on 18 lines**. That is **WITHDRAWN as an undercount with a named cause** [R18]: the selector required a `+` immediately *before* the ternary, so it matched `x+(c?'a':'')` and structurally could not see `(c?'a':'')+x`. Re-derived by a method that walks back to the matching `?` at the same bracket depth and accepts a `+` on **either** side: **116 ternaries on 93 lines, 38 appending occurrences on 31 lines**, of which 19 are the right-side form the first method missed. Antagonist A recounted independently with its own published script and got **115 / 41 on 34 lines** — three lines more than me (6875, 7264, 8003). Both figures are heuristics over a 9,000-line file and **the disagreement is itself the finding** [#411]: two commands that disagree by 3 of 34 lines are a better record than one that looks exact.

**WHAT THE CLASSIFICATION SETTLES, which does not depend on the exact denominator.** Of the appending occurrences, exactly **two** reach a box whose height is reserved rather than content-sized — both the puzzle verdict box, at `setPuzMsg` (search `Rank up — you reached` and `Checkmate — that works too!`) — and of those two, **one violates US-R47 and one satisfies it** by putting the news first. One more appends news to an *uncapped* box, the online and correspondence status line (search `you are out of time`), PARKED under R39. Everything else appends a detail rather than news, is a dev readout, or chooses between two values instead of appending.

**INPUTS — 10 measured states.** Geometries 375x761 (R19's settled figure for Kunal's phone, which the previous scoping never visited), 320x568 and 390x844, plus 375x730 and 375x568 on the driver state. Three inputs: the driver's own rank-up (68 cp, five geometries); curated **`c7`** at absolute index 720 (265 cp, three geometries); curated **`c13`** at 722 as the CONTROL (285 cp, 375x761). **390x844 is in scope and is not redundant** — the de-scoping in the first version of this case rested on the claim that width is the only thing that changes the wrap, and that is false: the reserved height is 109 at 320x568 and 375x761 and **153** at 390x844.

**THE ROUTE, because the first version named an input with no way to reach it.** `gates/drive/puzzles.js` state `rankup-continue` **cannot** produce the curated inputs: it seeds the first nine Novice puzzles and solves PZ[9], and `▶ Train next puzzle` takes the next *unsolved in index order*, so on that route the tenth solve in a tier is always tier-index start+9 — a generated puzzle with a 19-to-34-code-point explanation. The curated puzzles sit at Master-relative indices 33, 51, 52, 53 and 54. The reachable route, driven for real at #502: seed the **677** ids below Master-relative index 9 (668 pre-Master + 9 Master) so the tier stands at nine solves; `▶ Train next puzzle`, which enters at absolute index 677; then **43 taps of the `›` "Next puzzle" chevron** to index 720, asserting the header reads `721 / 890` and the goal `White to play — mate in 2 / Smothered mate`; then `Qg8+` as d5→g8 and `Nf7#` as h6→f7; then `Continue ▶`. For the `c13` control, 45 taps to index 722, then f2→g1, the `Knight` button in the promotion picker, c8→g4 and g4→d1.

**PASS CONDITION.** In the painted box located as `gates/regress/57-pz-solved-explanation.js` locates it — the leaf `div` whose text starts with the party popper:

  - **A** every **ink** code point of the ANNOUNCEMENT is painted inside the reserved box. Measured with a `Range` per code point clipped to the box's own rect, and **whitespace is counted but never credited** — the first version of this measurement credited all 10 spaces unmeasured and so reported "10 of 41" for a clause that was entirely invisible.
  - **B** the box hides nothing: `scrollHeight <= clientHeight`.
  - **C** BOTH clauses are on the frame, so satisfying the clause by deleting the announcement passes only when the celebration modal is separately asserted to carry it.
  - **D** the denominator is asserted before A is read: `announcementInk > 0`.
  - **E** THE INPUT ITSELF IS ASSERTED: the header reads the expected index and the goal card the expected motif, before any measurement. The first version of this case named an input it could not reach, and a gate written to it would have measured a different puzzle and gone green on a broken app.

**THE RESULT.** The driver's 68-cp verdict is whole at all five geometries (announcement ink 26 of 26, 0px hidden). **`c7` is cut**: box 109 against content 137 at 375x761 and 156 at 320x568, so **28px and 47px hidden**, announcement ink **0 of 26** at both, nothing of the news painted; at 390x844 the box is 153 against 178, 25px hidden, announcement ink **5 of 26**, the player seeing `⬆Rank`. **`c13` is WHOLE** — box 169 against content 169, 0px hidden, announcement ink 26 of 26 — **and that is the control that matters**, because `c13` has the *longer* verdict (285 cp against 265) and is the input the first version of this case wrongly named as the failing one. The cause is that the box's height is computed from the goal card: `c13`'s two-line goal reserves 169px, `c7`'s one-line goal reserves 109px.

**WHAT THIS CASE DOES NOT COVER.** It does not drive the alt-mate branch, which is unreachable on every long curated puzzle. It does not cover the second class member (`Checkmate — that works too!`), which satisfies the clause. It does not cover the online status line, parked under R39. It does not measure the celebration modal's own layout, only that the modal carries the news in full before it is dismissed. And it does not drive **antagonist A's cross-tier route** — a solve that completes tier T while higher tiers are already complete, so the announced rank is not the solved puzzle's tier and the clause can be the 41-code-point Grandmaster one; A derived it from `pzRank` returning the count of *consecutively* completed tiers, I have **not** reproduced it, and it is routed on the job rather than asserted here.

**NUMBERS I COULD NOT RECONCILE, recorded rather than smoothed over.** Antagonist B drove `c7` independently and reported the verdict as 267 cp and the announcement as 0 of 33; I measure **265 cp** and **0 of 26**, and the Master announcement is 36 code points of which 26 are ink, so 33 corresponds to neither. The verdict-length difference is a whitespace-collapsing convention; the denominator difference I did not resolve. Both of us measure the same verdict — **0 painted** — so the disagreement is in the denominator and not in the finding.

**GATE: NONE YET, AND THAT IS DELIBERATE [R17].** The job says "No gate file written: the test-authoring lane owns gate files", so #502 specified this case and measured it with a scratch probe held outside the committed tree. The natural home is a new block in `gates/regress/57-pz-solved-explanation.js`, which already locates this box and drives `rankup-continue`. **Its own assertion D is the one that would have caught this** — the reserved parent's `scrollHeight <= clientHeight` — and it is never handed an input that can fail it: the gate drives two verdict lengths out of 890 puzzles and neither is curated. That gate's header calls the rank-up concatenation "MEASURED AND NOT ASSERTED" for a reason that has **not** expired as the first version of this case claimed: it was written so that asserting it could not redden a correct fix of its parent job, and the right change is to hand assertion D a curated input, not to retire the note.

### TC-R63 (US-R36 clause (6)) — the filter header's controls reserve their space, so nothing under them moves when one appears

TC-R63  US-R36 clause (6) (#507), a control that can appear in the Review filter header occupies its space whether or not it is painted.  GATE `gates/regress/73-review-list-filter.js`, **block F driven at THREE geometries** plus **block G, added at #508, driven at the same three geometries over a FOUR-ACCOUNT fixture**, plus **C3/C3c** in block C for the coverage line's box and **C1b/C1c** for the sentence that sizes it; B7 and D2b were rewritten by this case and are counted under TC-R48.  **THE ASSERTION COUNT FOR BLOCK F IS CORRECTED HERE [#508, R18]: this line read "block F (10 assertions) driven at THREE geometries = 30 assertions" and block F emits EIGHT per geometry, 24 in all** — counted off a real run (`grep -c '^PASS F'`), not from the source. It was 10 when written and became 8 in the same build, because the same antagonist veto that folded F4c and F4d into F4 removed two assertions and nobody re-counted the case row. Block G adds SEVEN per geometry (G0, G0b, G1, G2a, G2, G3, G4) at TWO geometries, **14 in all**, and the file is **105** assertions — counted off a run, not from the source. It is not 18: block G first ran at three geometries and `se` was removed on measurement, see THE DENOMINATOR below.  GEOMETRIES: **320x568, 375x730 and 375x761**.  **THE INPUT SPACE HAS TWO AXES, NOT ONE** — geometry AND the number of connected accounts — and the second was added at #508 (see THE DENOMINATOR below).  INPUTS: the seven-game offline fixture of TC-R48 at TWO connected accounts for block F, and the SAME SEVEN GAMES redistributed over FOUR accounts for block G so every count on the screen is unchanged and the only variable is how many chips the row must wrap; `ct_ccuser` EMPTY so the auto-fetch effect never fires and api.chess.com is never asked for.

PASS CONDITIONS, each failing separately: (a) THE CLEAR CHIP IS IN THE LAYOUT ON THE UNFILTERED SCREEN and paints no ink there (F1, F1b); (b) THE ROW'S BOX DOES NOT CHANGE HEIGHT when the chip becomes painted, and its wrapped LINE COUNT does not change either — the mechanism behind the height rather than a second reading of it (F2, F3); (c) THE RESERVED BOX IS INERT — **and this clause is corrected [#508, R18]: it read "on all three counts that make it so, **asserted separately** because a regression can remove any one alone … (F4, **F4c, F4d**)", and F4c and F4d DO NOT EXIST.** The gate's own line 57 says so: a previous antagonist vetoed them as not three independent mechanisms, since `visibility:hidden` implies all three and each would have reddened a *correct* bundle achieving inertness another way [#391]. They were folded into F4 plus an explicit un-asserted `L.note`. **So there is NO assertion anywhere, in block F or block G, that the ghost is out of the tap path, out of the accessibility tree or out of the tab order** — what IS asserted is the load-bearing property, that the box is full-size and paints nothing (F4, F4b, G1, G4). The three properties were independently MEASURED sound at four accounts by #508's shipped-surface antagonist (tap and mouse click inert; 30 Tab presses never focus it; zero of 614 accessibility nodes match /clear/i), which is evidence and not coverage. #508 corrected the block-F assertion count in the line directly above this one from the same `grep -c` and left this clause standing; it is fixed here; (d) THE COVERAGE LINE HOLDS ITS BOX for as long as a grade filter is on and drops only its ink (C3, C3c).

THE DENOMINATOR IS THE GEOMETRY LIST, AND IT IS WHY THIS CASE RUNS AT 320x568. **MEASURED on main's bundle `ebb88c8f76f7` and on `6683fe5db924` with ONE probe (`6683fe5db924` is a SUPERSEDED #507 bundle, kept because it is what the probe ran on; the bundle that SHIPS is `28f1ff838440` over source `83610da7d7c0`, and the px figures are unaffected because neither rebuild touched the chip row [#508, R18]): the chip row grows 94px/2 lines to 144px/3 lines at 320x568 and DOES NOT MOVE AT ALL at 375x730 or 375x761 (94px, 2 lines, both states).** So a case pinned only to this file's existing 375x730 could not have failed on the defect it is written for — which the control proves rather than argues: in NC-NORESERVE, **F2 and F3 each redden EXACTLY ONCE across the three geometries**, at `se` alone. That is the #375 trap (a gate exercising the fixed path twice) arriving through the geometry list instead of through a worker pool. Per #505's instruction, 375x761 is **added** here and 375x730 is **not substituted**, so no existing pin in the file moves.

**THE DENOMINATOR IS NOT ONLY THE GEOMETRY LIST, AND THE SENTENCE ABOVE IS THE EVIDENCE THAT SAYS SO WITHOUT DRAWING THE CONCLUSION [#508].** Read it again: in NC-NORESERVE *F2 and F3 each redden exactly once, at `se` alone*. The other side of that measurement is that **at 375x730 and 375x761 those two assertions PASS ON A BUNDLE WITH THE RESERVE REMOVED** — they have no discriminating power at Kunal's own width, and the geometry ladder cannot give them any, because the figure was taken at ONE ACCOUNT COUNT. The account chips share this row, so the number of connected accounts moves the wrap boundary exactly as the width does. **MEASURED at #508 on the shipped bundle and on the one-change control, three geometries x two account counts:**

| | 2 accounts | 4 accounts |
|---|---|---|
| **320x568** | control jumps 94->144 (**50px**); fix holds 144 | both 144 at rest; neither jumps |
| **375x730** | control 94 both states (**no power**); fix 94 | control jumps 94->144 (**50px**); fix holds 144 |
| **375x761** | control 94 both states (**no power**); fix 94 | control jumps 94->144 (**50px**); fix holds 144 |

**Block G is that cell**, and on the control its G2 and G3 redden at exactly the two geometries where F2 and F3 cannot.

**AND THE VARIABLE IS THE TOTAL CHIP WIDTH, NOT THE ACCOUNT COUNT — a correction to #508's own first wording on an upheld antagonist finding [R18].** The withdrawn sentence said *"the four-account state is the only cell at 375 where this clause can fail at all"* and *"the account count moves the wrap boundary just as the width does"*. The account chips share the row, so what crosses the boundary is how wide the chips are in total; the count is only a proxy. **Measured: the relation is NOT MONOTONIC in the count** — the row is 94px/2 lines at one, two AND three accounts, 144px/3 at four, and an antagonist measured it back to a no-op at five with the labels it used. A four-account state with SHORT labels (`al`, `be`, `ga`, `de`) is 94px/2 lines and the reserve costs nothing there, so **G0 passing is not sufficient** — which is why **G0b** now asserts the real condition (the Clear chip sits on a wrapped line of its own) in its own `L.say`, with the label widths in the payload. Four accounts with THESE label widths is one instance of the condition, not the condition. **THE PART WORTH CARRYING IS NOT THE GAP, IT IS HOW VISIBLE IT WAS:** the decomposition was already published here, in this section, correctly, and cited as the reason the geometry ladder earns its place. The number was right and in the right document; the inference that something OTHER than geometry must give those two cells their power was never made. A measurement that proves an axis is exhausted reads very like one that proves the axis is working.

AND THE ANTI-VACUITY GUARD IS ITS OWN ASSERTION. F2 and F3 compare two states, so both are vacuously true if the second state never arrived. **F2a** asserts the grade chip really went on — the row count changed AND Clear is now painted — before either height is compared [the #385 rule: when an assertion says X is unchanged in state S, assert first that S was actually reached].

IT FAILED BEFORE THE FIX, AND THERE ARE THREE CONTROLS, each cut from the shipping source by ONE change and each reddening a **DISJOINT** set:
• **NC-NORESERVE** — the Clear chip returned to its shipped conditional render. #507 published bundle `a7dec72c28a5` at **73 PASS / 20 FAIL** red at F1×3, F1b×3, F2×1, F3×1, F4×3, F4b×3, F4c×3, F4d×3; **that is WITHDRAWN [#508, R18]**. Re-derived on the shipping source: bundle `9f79fc2fea1d`, **76 PASS / 14 FAIL**, red at F1×3, F1b×3, F2×1, F3×1, F4×3, F4b×3. **F4c and F4d are no longer assertions at all** — antagonist A vetoed them as not three independent mechanisms, since `visibility:hidden` implies all three and each would redden a *correct* bundle achieving inertness another way [#391]; they were folded into F4 plus an explicit un-asserted note. The control's id set shrank because the gate got honester.
• **NC-NOCOVRESERVE** — the coverage line returned to `_gradeOn&&_ungr>0&&`. #507 published bundle `757fb8c08773` at **91 PASS / 2 FAIL** red at C3 and C3c only; **WITHDRAWN [#508, R18]**. Re-derived on the shipping source: bundle `5680506bfb5a`, **86 PASS / 4 FAIL**, red at C3, C3c, **C3d and C3f** — the latter two are new assertions that arrived with antagonist B's painted-ink change, so the gate is stronger and its control reports it.
• **NC-WIDETEMPLATE** — `_narrowed` forced false so the unnarrowed wording prints over a narrowed set. #507 published bundle `5b24fbf005b3` at **92 PASS / 1 FAIL**, red at C1c only, and claimed **C1b PASSED on that control** as "the measured proof that C1c and not C1b is the discriminating assertion". **THAT CLAIM IS FALSE ON THE SHIPPING TREE AND IS WITHDRAWN, not merely restated [#508, R18]**: re-derived on the shipping source, bundle `bbefc99c3eec` scores **88 PASS / 2 FAIL** and **C1b REDDENS** alongside C1c. So BOTH halves of the C1 pair discriminate here. The conclusion #507 drew — that an alternation over both templates would pin nothing — remains true *as a statement about alternations* and now rests on C1c's own message rather than on C1b's verdict [#388]. The gate is stronger than #507 claimed and its published reasoning was wrong; both are recorded, not just the flattering one.

ONE ASSERTION WAS WITHDRAWN BEFORE IT SHIPPED, recorded because the reason generalises. F4's first draft PRESSED the ghosted chip's own coordinates and asserted the screen did not change. It **cannot fail**: the chip is ghosted only when no filter is on, so `_clearAll()` is a no-op in that state and a fully live control would have left the screen identical. A trial bundle with `pointerEvents:'auto'` restored would have passed it. Replaced by the three property assertions in (c). Found by asking what the control bundle would print [#418: ask what your program would print on a case you already know is fine].

| bundle | tree | geometries | result |
|---|---|---|---|
Every row below is **#508's re-derivation on the shipping source `83610da7d7c0`**. #507's four published rows were measured before two upheld antagonist vetoes changed both the gate and chess.jsx, and are withdrawn above [R18].

**THE NC-NOGHOST ROW HAS NOW BEEN PUBLISHED THREE TIMES IN ONE BUILD AND THE FIRST TWO ARE WITHDRAWN [#508, R18]. The sequence is kept because it is the clearest record this file has of a sum check being necessary and not sufficient.** (1) **82/27** — measured truthfully against block G's FIRST draft, whose G0 was a conjunct; splitting G0 moved three reds to passes. (2) **85/24** — measured against the gate after the split but before block G was restricted to the two 375 geometries. (3) **83/22, sum 105** — the shipping figure, after an antagonist showed and block G's own new denominator guard then confirmed that `se` had no discriminating power at four accounts. **ALL THREE SUM CORRECTLY to the gate they were run on (109, 109, 105), so the sum check never fired** — it cannot see a red moving to a pass, nor a geometry leaving a block. What caught (1) was reading WHICH ids failed and why; what caught (2) was a new assertion failing on the shipping bundle. Neither was arithmetic.

**TWO THINGS THE BLOCK F REDS CONFIRM INDEPENDENTLY.** First, the 14 block-F reds here are id-for-id the set NC-NORESERVE reports (F1x3, F1bx3, F2x1, F3x1, F4x3, F4bx3), re-derived from a control cut against a DIFFERENT source (`a6e49fb93cdd` rather than `83610da7d7c0`) — so #508's re-derivation of #507's figure reproduces at one remove. Second, F2 and F3 still redden only at `se`, which is the measurement this case already carried; what is new is that **G2 and G3 redden at kunal730 and kunal761 and nowhere else**, so the two blocks' discriminating cells are disjoint and together they cover all three geometries.

**EVERY ROW SUMS TO THE GATE'S OWN ASSERTION COUNT**, which is the cheap check that they share one denominator — **91** for the five rows measured against the #507 gate and **105** for #508's two rows. Check a row against the gate it was run on, never against the newest number. **FIVE DISTINCT SOURCE MUTATIONS, NOT SIX — a correction to #508's own first wording here [R18]:** NC-NOGHOST and NC-NORESERVE are THE SAME one-line change re-cut against a later source, and NC-NOGHOST's red set is a strict SUPERSET of NC-NORESERVE's. The sets differ because the GATE grew, not because the controls are independent, so "six controls, six distinct red sets" overstated the evidence. NC-NOGHOST is the only control that reddens block G.

**AND THE ASSERTION COUNT FELL, 109 to 105, WHICH NORMALLY MEANS WEAKENING AND HERE MEANS THE OPPOSITE.** Block G first ran at three geometries; `se` was removed after its own new denominator guard G0b FAILED there, with `otherTops` [603.8, 653.8, 703.8] against `clearTop` 703.8 — the Clear chip SHARES line 3 at 320. Measured: at 320x568 with four accounts the row is 144px/3 lines on the shipping bundle AND on the control, so the reserve costs nothing there and G2/G3 had no discriminating power; block G at `se` was four assertions duplicating F1/F1b/F4/F4b plus two that pass on the only control. An antagonist argued this from the diff and the guard then proved it from the inside. **Six assertions left and no coverage did:** F2/F3 redden at `se`, G2/G3 at kunal730 and kunal761, cells disjoint, all three geometries covered.

**ONE OF BLOCK G's OWN ASSERTIONS WAS A CONJUNCT AND ITS AUTHOR'S CONTROL CAUGHT IT BEFORE IT SHIPPED [#508].** G0 is the denominator guard — it exists to prove the four-account state actually rendered — and its first draft read `acctChips.length===4 && pre.chips.length===8`. On NC-NOGHOST it went RED, not because the fixture failed but because the Clear chip is absent at rest on that bundle, so the total is 7. A denominator guard that reddens while its denominator is sound reports two facts through one verdict, which is the trap this file's own header warns about and that #493's antagonists found three live instances of. Split so G0 asserts the account count alone and the total rides in the payload; G1 is what detects the missing reserve. Found by running the control and reading which ids failed and why, not by review.

**#508 CLEARED AN UPHELD VETO THAT HAD BEEN SILENTLY UN-FIXED BETWEEN TWO OF #507's OWN COMMITS, and this is the most reusable thing on this case.** `bebd6ef` repaired E7b to `ungFound && ungInk` against a bundle that carried `visibility:_ungr>0?'visible':'hidden'` on the coverage line. `880fda5` — part 3, antagonist B's painted-ink recommendation — then **deleted that toggle**. Nothing in the shipping tree sets `visibility` on the element or any ancestor, and visibility is inherited, so `ungInk` became identical to `ungFound` and the predicate collapsed back to the exact one that had been vetoed two commits earlier. **Measured, not argued:** on NC-UNGR, E7b *passed* with payload `{"ungraded":"All 7 games are graded.","graded":null,"total":null}` — the defect certified by the assertion written to forbid it. Re-pointed at the warning branch (`graded<total`, which is C2's shape) and it now reddens. The lesson is not "re-run your controls"; it is that **a repair and the mechanism it depends on can be in the same build, two commits apart, and nothing mechanical connects them.**

**#508 FOUND TWO UNCOVERED STATES ON THIS SCREEN AND NEITHER IS FIXED HERE. Both are measured, both are filed, and saying so
is the point of this entry rather than a confession.**

**(1) `n===0` PRINTS A COMPLETENESS CLAIM OVER AN EMPTY SET, and it is reachable.** With the name box on `zzzznosuch` and a
grade chip on — two accounts or four, at 375x730 — `[data-ct="glist-ungraded"]` reads **"All the 0 games this filter is
looking at are graded."** Measured by #508's shipped-surface antagonist and reproduced by the build. US-R36 is the story about
never presenting a partly computed tally as if it were complete, and this states completeness about nothing. Block C has
`C3e`/`C3e0` and a whole negative control for the `n===1` case, added on a previous antagonist's finding; **nobody drove
`n===0`.** `n===1` is correct — "All the 1 game this filter is looking at is graded." — so the plural helper is sound and the
gap is the zero input. Filed on `jobs/review-list-filter-and-search-2026-09-23`; the fix is one branch in `_covTxt` and it is

**(2) THE RESERVED CLEAR CHIP IS OCCLUDED BY THE FIXED TAB BAR AT FOUR ACCOUNTS, AND THE GATE CANNOT SEE IT BY CONSTRUCTION.**
At 375x730 the painted chip's box runs 688.9–732.9 against a fixed bottom tab bar at 674 on a 730 viewport: 41.1px of 44px
covered, 2.9px off-screen. `elementFromPoint` at its own centre returns `SPAN:"Home"` and a **real finger tap does not clear
the filter — it navigates to Home.** At one, two and three accounts the same tap works. **Why no assertion:** the defect is
PRE-EXISTING on main (#508's bundle is byte-identical to main's but for twelve build-stamp bytes, and the control shares it),

`gatemanifest check` report the missing class gate every run. It is the first instance of that class NOT on a short screen —
the other four are 568-tall or landscape — because the trigger is a growing content row, not a short viewport.

It now runs at the n===1 state antagonist B actually measured (account beta plus the pre-existing name box on `opp6`, where the fixture's `g6` has no tally), with **C3e0** as its own separate non-vacuity assertion rather than a conjunct.
