NOTE ON THE ID. **TC-R63 was checked free by grep over the whole repository before use, and TC-R62 was NOT free** — it is US-R50's modal-reachability case on gate 74, landed at #505. The highest `TC-R` id in this file is 61, so taking "the next one" would have collided; the id space is not contiguous and must be grepped, not incremented.

### TC-R62 (US-R50) — a modal dialog's controls are REACHABLE, not merely painted

**ARRIVED AT #498** for the remainder of `jobs/the-lesson-cta-centre-hit-tests-to-the-lesson-footer-and-a-tap-closes-the-lesson-at-568-tall-2026-10-04` (band 16, P0), filed separately as `jobs/the-lesson-intro-card-can-be-taller-than-the-viewport-and-nothing-scrolls-it-2026-10-08`. US-R50 already carried the clause; this is its unmet half. The id was **reserved on the tracker** by that job, not cleared by grep — which is the trap TC-R51's and TC-R60's own notes warn about, because a reserved id lives on the tracker and is invisible to the tree.

**GATE** `gates/regress/74-lesson-card-hit-area.js`, assertions **B1b, B1c (note), B1d, B5, B1bG, B1bG2, C0**. Same file as TC-R60 deliberately: it is the same element, the same six driven lessons and the same hold, and a second gate would re-pay the ~90 seconds of driving to assert a sibling property.

**INPUTS.** Two tiers, and the second is what makes it a class rather than an instance.
- **The gate: 6 named lessons x 3 geometries = 18 driven intro cards.** `se` (320x568), `short375` (375x568), `kunal730` (375x730).
- **The class sweep: 170 lesson cards — every row of all four groups — at 320x568, one browser, about 7 minutes.** This is the tier that found the regression described below, and a three-card check would not have.

**PASS CONDITION, and it is a REACHABILITY test and not an overflow test.** Every interactive control of the live modal layer must be **either** fully inside the viewport at rest, **or** brought fully inside it by scrolling an ancestor *a finger can scroll* — computed `overflow-y` of `auto` or `scroll` AND `scrollHeight > clientHeight + 1` — scrolled **for real**, with the control's rect **re-read afterwards** and every `scrollTop` restored. **Never `scrollIntoView`**, which happily scrolls an `overflow:hidden` box and so reports "reachable" on a build with the scroller removed; that is the exact false pass `gates/regress/40-reachability.js` records as its own first version's bug. A control taller than the viewport is reported and not counted as unreachable, so a larger accessibility font cannot manufacture a false red.

**AND THE PASS CONDITION IS NOT `fullyOnScreen`, WHICH IS THE WHOLE REASON THIS CASE EXISTS SEPARATELY FROM TC-R60.** #497's B1b counted controls whose rect is not entirely inside the viewport, with a per-card ceiling of 2 — Petroff's CTA and its close ✕. **Measured on #498's bundle, Petroff STILL has exactly two such controls** (its CTA, and a Related-lessons chip). So that assertion reads `2 <= 2` on both the broken and the fixed bundle and is **incapable of distinguishing unreachable ink from below-the-fold ink**. It was watching the right card and measuring the wrong property. Below the fold inside a scroller is **correct** under US-R50's own words — *"a dialog whose content scrolls satisfies that by scrolling"* — so it is reported by B1c as a NOTE and never as a verdict; a red there would redden a correct tree the first time any lesson's text gains a line.

**THE MECHANISM IS ASSERTED TOO (B1d), not only the symptom.** #498's fix is `maxHeight:'100vh'` + `overflowY:'auto'` on the card's panel, so B1d asserts the panel's own rect is entirely inside the viewport. On main it read **-33.27..601.28, height 634.55 in a 568 viewport**, off *both* ends, because the overlay is `alignItems:'center'`.

**ANTI-VACUITY (B5 per geometry, B1bG2 globally).** B1b and B1d can only fail where a card's content exceeds the viewport. **No card does at 375x730** — the tallest of the six measures 607.5 in 730 — so their green there is a positive control and not evidence, and B5 says so as a NOTE with no PASS emitted. B1bG2 is the hard global guard: at least one card in the run must have its panel **at** the cap and scrolling, so adding a tall geometry can never silently disarm the pair. Measured on #498: `se/Petroff scrollRoom 67` and `short375/Petroff scrollRoom 5`.

**EVIDENCE IT FAILED BEFORE THE FIX — the control is free, because it is the tree this build was cut from.**

```
git show <#497 sha>:app.js > gates/.trial/mainbundle.js      # md5 e60f12585339
CT_APP=gates/.trial/mainbundle.js CT_74_GEOS=se node gates/regress/74-lesson-card-hit-area.js
```

| bundle | what it is | geometries | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| `95ee23d86752` | **#498's tree. HELD AND NEVER SHIPPED** — see the correction below | se, short375, kunal730 | 223 pass, 0 fail **AS #498's GATE READ IT** |
| `e60f12585339` | #497's shipped bundle: no cap, no scroller | se | **70 pass, 5 FAIL** |
| `598cbe4857d6` | the shipped bundle with **only `overflowY:'auto'` deleted** — capped but NOT scrolling | se | **70 pass, 4 FAIL** |
| `a210b786bfe4` | #498's bundle with **only the `onScroll` hook deleted** — scrolling but NOT holding | se | 79 pass, 1 FAIL (D2) |

**CORRECTED AT #505 [R18]. THE ROW ABOVE FOR `95ee23d86752` SAID "the SHIPPED tree" AND IT IS A BUNDLE THAT MUST NOT SHIP.** `gates/held-trees.tsv` carries that exact md5 with the reason: *gate 74 is RED on it by design (77 pass / 2 FAIL at 320x568, D1 and D2)*. Two committed files on one tree gave the same md5 opposite verdicts, and the one a later run reads for this gate's controls was the one saying the held bundle was green — #450's shape with the sign flipped, a false clearance landed on `main` rather than a warning lost on a branch. Found by #505's antagonist A from the diff door. The 223/0 figure is not withdrawn as a *reading* — it is what #498's gate scored on its own tree before its last commit made D assert the reader's real gesture — but it is not a clearance and the word "SHIPPED" is removed.

**#505's OWN CONTROLS, which are the ones to use from here.** Three single-variable bundles cut from the shipped one, each reddening a disjoint set, which is what says the three assertions measure three things:

| bundle | what it is, relative to the shipped bundle | geometries | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| **shipped** | — | se, short375, kunal730 | **227 pass, 0 fail** |
| `nc-nocapture` | `onScrollCapture` → `onScroll`, **7 bytes**, nothing else | se | **84 pass, 1 FAIL** — D2 only |
| `nc-unguarded` | the panel-has-room guard removed, capture kept | se | **84 pass, 1 FAIL** — F1 only |
| `nc-touchaction` | `touch-action:'none'` on the panel and both inner blocks | se | **79 pass, 5 FAIL** — B1b, B1bG, C0, D1b, D2 |

The md5s are deliberately **not** pinned in this row: #505 rebuilt four times on upheld vetoes, so any md5 written here would have gone stale three times. They are in `gates/regress/74-lesson-card-hit-area.js`'s own CONTROLS block, which is rewritten against the final bundles at the end of the run.

Main's five reds are B1b (2 unreachable, both `scrollers=0`, both `movedBy=0`), B1d (panel does not fit), B1e2 (the close ✕ unreachable), B1bG and B1bG2 — all five in the overflow block, while **A3, B1, C0 and C1 stay GREEN**, because #497 fixed the hit test and not the overflow.

**THE THIRD CONTROL IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS, AND IT EXISTS BECAUSE THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS GATE SCORED 56 pass / 1 fail ON IT WITH EVERY NEW ASSERTION GREEN.** A 20-character deletion from the shipped bundle leaves the panel capped but not scrolling — the defect intact, CTA at 573.55 with zero user-scrollable ancestors — and B1b, B1d, B1bG, B5 and B1bG2 all passed. Two independent causes, both found by antagonist A and both fixed before the push:

1. **The reachability assertion was reading the hit-test denominator**, which is filtered to controls *intersecting* the viewport. The fix moved the CTA from 540.28 (intersecting, counted) to 573.55 (entirely below, **dropped**), so B1b read "2 → 0" partly because the element under test left its own input set. B1b now reads a separate set — every interactive descendant of the panel, size-filtered only, never viewport-filtered — and **B1a2 asserts the CTA is IN that set by name**, which is the assertion that would have caught this where a non-emptiness guard would not.
2. **`capBound` counted `scrollHeight > clientHeight` alone**, which is true of a capped panel whose `overflow-y` is `visible` and which scrolls nowhere, so B5 and B1bG2 asserted "at the cap **and scrolling**" about a panel that does not scroll. It now requires `isUserScroller` too.

On `598cbe4857d6` **B1d PASSES** — the panel genuinely does fit — while B1b reddens. That is what says B1b and B1d are not two names for one measurement: one is about the box, the other about whether a finger can reach what is inside it.

**A COST THIS FIX HAS AND THE BUILD DID NOT ORIGINALLY NAME, found by antagonist A.** The close ✕ is `position:absolute; top:9` *inside* the panel that is now the scroller, so it scrolls with the content. On the shipped bundle at 320x568: at rest the ✕ is `10..40`, fully on screen; scrolled to the end (67 of 67) it is `-57..-27`, 0.00px on screen, and `elementFromPoint` at its centre returns nothing. **There is no single scroll position showing both the ✕ and the CTA:** the ✕ needs `scrollTop ≤ 39`, the CTA needs `≥ 49.55`. That is a COST, not a defect, and the distinction is US-R50's own — the clause requires each control to BE REACHABLE, and the ✕ is reachable by scrolling back up, which B1e2 verifies on every card. What the clause does not ask for, and what is therefore not asserted, is one position serving both. Carried as `jobs/the-intro-cards-close-x-scrolls-out-of-the-card-it-closes-2026-10-08` with `position:sticky` as the candidate remedy.

**THE CLASS SWEEP, and its numbers are the deliverable [R06].** Predicate per card: hold nothing (the measurement is inside the 4000ms auto-dismiss, so no hold is needed for a rect read), then for every control of the panel compute `restFullyOnScreen` and `REACHABLE` as defined above.

| bundle | cards | unreachable-control cards | below-fold-but-reachable cards | tallest panel in a 568 viewport |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `e60f12585339` (#497) | 170 | **1** — Openings/45 Petroff, 2 controls | 1 | 634.55 (overflows both ends) |
| `0cc8d3dc196f` (#498, first attempt) | 170 | 0 | **2** — regressed Gambits/50 | 532 |
| `8f25da67c4e3` (#498, shipped) | 170 | **0** | **1** — Petroff only, as main | 568 (exactly fills it) |

**THE MIDDLE ROW IS THE POINT AND IT IS KEPT DELIBERATELY.** My first cap was `calc(100vh - 36px)`, respecting the overlay's own `padding:18`. It is tighter than the *viewport*, and the defect is about the viewport: it fixed Petroff and moved **Gambits row 50's CTA from 510.94..554.94, fully on screen on main, to 532.86..576.86, below the fold** — a card with nothing wrong with it made worse to fix a broken one, which is #398's rule exactly ("the damage moved to the row nobody was asserting over"). `100vh` binds only where the panel would otherwise leave the screen. **It was caught only by the 170-card tier**; the gate's six lessons do not include Gambits row 50 and went green on both caps.

**C0/C1 CHANGED WITH THE FIX RATHER THAN BEING DELETED**, which is CLAUDE.md's rule for an assertion that pins a position. C1 taps the CTA's own centre for real. On #498 at 320x568 that centre is at y 595.55 in a 568 viewport — below the fold — so the unconditional click tapped nothing and C1 went red on a correct tree (measured: 141 pass / 1 fail). It now **scrolls what a finger can scroll first, re-reads the rect, and taps the new centre**, with C0 asserting separately that the centre is inside the viewport when the tap is made so that a failure says which half went. **C0 tests the CENTRE, not the full rect:** requiring the full rect reddened main, where the centre (562.28) is perfectly tappable while the bottom (584.28) is not, and that both produced a hit-test red from an overflow control and skipped C1 on the one bundle whose hit-test behaviour C1 exists to pin. On #498 Petroff reports `scrollNeeded:true, scrollers:1`, the centre moves to 537.55, and the real tap dismisses the card.

**WHAT IS NOT COVERED, listed because absence is the hardest thing to measure.** The class sweep ran at **320x568 only**; 375x679, 390x844, 430x932 and landscape are not swept, and landscape is the likely worst case because the card is centre-anchored with vh-based inner caps. Larger accessibility font sizes are not measured and are the obvious route from a clean lesson into this population. The other two members of #497's modal class — the puzzle celebration and the promotion picker — are not swept for overflow.

**AND THE CAP ALONE WAS NOT THE WHOLE CASE: BLOCK D (D0c, D1, D2, D3) EXISTS BECAUSE THE CAP MADE ITS OWN BUTTON NEED A GESTURE THAT KILLED THE CARD.** `introHoldRef` is set only by the panel's `onClick` (`chess.jsx`, search `introHoldRef`), and the 4000 ms auto-dismiss is armed in the `useEffect` that sets `introHoldRef.current=false` (located by symbol, not by line: #505's antagonist A measured the previous citation, which named `chess.jsx` at line 2850, as 69 lines stale (written here in prose rather than in the `file:line` form on purpose: the form is what the register's line-citation counter counts, and a WITHDRAWN citation quoted as history should not consume a headroom slot that the #486 payload bought to stop this audit reddening every build that touches claude/stories), and a line number in a document that cites a file it does not live in goes stale on every edit to that file). **A touch drag that scrolls fires no click.** So once the cap put Petroff's CTA below the fold, the only route to it was a gesture that did not hold the card. Measured with real CDP finger drags at 320x568: the panel reaches `scrollTop` 67 — **THREE drags with the 80px gesture this gate actually drives, not two** [R18, #505; "two" was measured with the 120px-from-y=420 gesture this gate's own comment rejects as "not the gesture a reader makes", and was published with no drag length or start point, which is #411]. Child 0→73, child 73→97, then panel 0→67 and the CTA's centre 595.55 → 528.55, so the scroll itself works; the card is **gone at +4600 ms anyway**; `elementFromPoint` at that centre returns a lesson-footer glyph; and a real tap there moves the lesson note from `"2. Nf3 — White develops…"` to `"2… Nf6 — Instead of defending…"`. **The demo stepped.** That is #497's own player-visible symptom returned by timing instead of by paint order, and it was a genuine regression: on main the CTA's centre is 562.28, on screen and tappable.

**THE REMEDY THIS PARAGRAPH USED TO PRESCRIBE IS THE ONE #505 REPLACED, AND THE SENTENCE IS WITHDRAWN [R18].** It read: *"The remedy is `onScroll` setting the same ref the `onClick` already sets."* `onScroll` **cannot** be the remedy, because the `scroll` event DOES NOT BUBBLE and the panel is not the card's only scroller — the idea block carries `maxHeight:40vh` and the plans block `26vh`, each with its own `overflowY:auto`, so a reader's drag on the body text scrolls the INNER block (room 97 against the panel's 67) and the panel's own `onScroll` never fires. The two-drag case reached the panel and passed; the ONE-drag case, which is what a reader does, did not, which is why #498's own last commit left D RED on its branch. **THE SHIPPED REMEDY IS `onScrollCapture`**, which listens at the root and replays the CAPTURE path through every ancestor of the target.

**AND THE CAPTURE FORM HAD TO BE NARROWED BEFORE IT COULD SHIP, on an upheld veto from #505's shipped-surface antagonist.** Unguarded, it fires on ANY descendant scroll on ANY card — including every card whose button is already fully on screen and whose panel has nowhere to scroll. MEASURED on Openings row 20, Queen's Gambit, at 320x568, where the panel's room is **0** and the CTA sits fully on screen at 495.56..539.56 in a 568 viewport: scrolling its one scrollable child by 55px with no click left the card up at **+4600, +12000 and +20000 ms**, over a demo that had autoplayed to its end, where a bundle with no hook dismissed it at +4600. Swept over all 97 Openings rows at 320x568: **80** have a scrollable child and a panel with no room, **1** has both (Petroff), **16** have neither — so the unguarded hook made a latent fault routine on 80 of 97 cards. The shipped hook therefore holds the card only where `scrollHeight > clientHeight + 1` on the panel itself, which is exactly where something is below the fold and a gesture is needed. **Block F asserts that, and `nc-unguarded` is its control.**

**D's ASSERTIONS, and why each exists.** **D0c** is the precondition: this block reaches the card through `openPlain`, which opens the lesson row and touches nothing, so `introHoldRef` is false going in. **D1 IS A NOTE AT #505 AND NO LONGER A VERDICT [R18].** This row used to call it *"the denominator — a real finger drag must actually have moved the panel (0 → 67 of 67)"*, and #498's own last commit had already repurposed it away from being a denominator into a hard assertion that one drag brings the CTA into the viewport. Measured on the shipped/control pair: D1 reads **identically on both bundles at both geometries**, to the digit (se panel 0→0 of 67, CTA centre 595.546875 in 568; short375 panel 0→0 of 5, centre 534.171875), so it has **zero discriminating power** for this fix — capture changes which element HEARS a scroll, not which element SCROLLS — and at short375 it passes as a positive control. Its property, one-gesture reachability, is stricter than US-R50's own words (*"a dialog whose content scrolls satisfies that by scrolling"*) and is the deliverable of `jobs/the-intro-cards-primary-button-should-not-be-below-its-own-fold-2026-10-08`. The measurement is still printed every run; only the verdict is gone. **D1b** is the denominator that replaced it: the one drag actually scrolled *something* inside the card (se panel 0 / deepest child 72-73 across runs, short375 panel 0 / child 34), so D2 is a statement about surviving a scroll and not about surviving a non-event. **D2** is the property: the card is still up at +4600 ms, past the dismiss. **D3** asserts the panel's computed `overflow-x` is pinned to `hidden`, at *every* geometry, because setting `overflow-y:auto` makes the other axis compute to `auto` and that defect is width-dependent and invisible at 320. **D3a** is a NOTE printing what pinning `overflow-x` costs — the panel's hidden width, 0 at all three geometries this gate drives, 35 at 256 wide and 74 at 213 — which is owed correction (1) from #498's antagonist C. **D2G** is the hard global anti-vacuity guard, the shape `B3G` and `B1bG2` already use: at least one geometry in the run must have put D2 to a real scroll gesture, so a geometry set where the cap never binds cannot report D2 green in silence. Where the panel has no scroll room D1b/D2 are skipped **as a NOTE with no PASS emitted**, because there is no gesture to hold and their green would be a positive control.

**BLOCK F (F0b, F0d, F0e, F1, F1G) IS NEW AT #505 AND IT ASSERTS THE OTHER SIDE OF THE HOLD.** F1: on a card whose panel has NO scroll room and whose button is already on screen, a descendant scroll with no click must leave the 4000 ms auto-dismiss alone. F0d asserts the precondition that makes F1 a statement about an *unneeded* hold — the CTA is already fully on screen — and F0e asserts the denominator, that a child actually scrolled. F1G is the global guard that the population exists in the run at all, which is precisely how #505's first fix regressed 80 of 97 cards with this gate green. **F scrolls programmatically and says so**, rather than claiming a finger it did not use — the mistake antagonist A caught in C0/C1 — because the question F asks is about the hold MECHANISM ("a scroll happened, no click happened; is the card held?") and a `scrollTop` assignment fires a real scroll event and no click. It is also the instrument that settled a disagreement: the antagonist reported a finger drag scrolling this card's plans block 0→55 and #505 could not reproduce that gesture over two attempts at two drag speeds, while the mechanism reproduced first time on both bundles. A finger-driven F is owed and is named in NOT CHECKED.

**AND B1b's REACHABILITY FILTER NOW READS `touch-action` [#505, upheld veto].** It filtered scrollable ancestors on computed `overflow-y` and room and nothing else, then assigned `scrollTop` — so it answered a question about what SCRIPT can scroll while every message built on it said *"what a FINGER can scroll"*. Proved on `nc-touchaction`, the shipped bundle with `touch-action:'none'` added to the panel and both inner blocks: six real finger drags leave every `scrollTop` at 0 and Petroff's CTA centre never moves from 595.55 in a 568 viewport, so a player can never reach that card's own button — and the gate scored **78 pass / 2 fail with B1b, B1d, B1bG, B1bG2, C0 and C1 ALL GREEN**, C1 printing "the card was scrolled first, as a finger would have to: 1 finger-scrollable ancestor" about a finger that scrolls nothing. This is this gate's own documented trap (a) — *"script is not so limited, and `scrollIntoView()` happily scrolls an `overflow:hidden` box"* — with `touch-action` as a second property of the same shape. The filter now walks the ancestor chain for the effective `touch-action` (the intersection down the chain, not a property of one element), and the same test was added to **both** copies of the walk, because fixing only `userScrollables` left C0 passing from the uncorrected copy in block C in the same run. On the shipped bundle nothing changes — computed `touch-action` is `auto` throughout — which is the point: a guard against a future tree, proved able to fire rather than asserted sound.

**D's FIRST VERSION WAS VACUOUS AND THIS IS THE THIRD INSTANCE OF THAT TRAP IN ONE BUILD.** It reached the card with `openHeld`, which **clicks the panel** — and the panel's `onClick` is one of the two things that sets `introHoldRef` — so the card was already held before the drag, and **D2 passed on the hook-deleted bundle at 79 pass / 0 fail.** The gate's own setup was satisfying the property under test. #385's rule is the remedy and is now followed: when an assertion says "X survives in state S", reach S by the route the *player* takes, not the route the harness finds shortest.

**ONE MORE CONTROL CAME BACK NOT-OURS, and it is recorded because claiming it would have been easy.** A player who never touches the card also loses it at 4000 ms, and their next tap at the CTA's position also steps the demo — measured **identically on main's own bundle `e60f12585339`**. So that path is main's behaviour, unchanged by this build, which neither caused nor fixes it. It is a product question about whether an informational card should auto-dismiss at all, filed as `jobs/the-intro-card-dismisses-itself-under-a-reader-and-the-next-tap-steps-the-demo-2026-10-08` and routed for the Desk rather than decided here.

**HARNESS TRAP, ESTABLISHED AND NOT TO BE REDISCOVERED.** `CDP Input.synthesizeScrollGesture` is **inert in this container** — 0 px produced against 6124 px of available root scroll, three times at three geometries. `page.mouse.wheel` works, and a manual `Input.dispatchTouchEvent` touchStart/Move/End sequence works. Any future gate claiming "a finger can scroll this" must not use `synthesizeScrollGesture`; `fingerDrag` in gate 74 is a working implementation, and D prints its tool-check against a known scroller before emitting any verdict.

**THE SWEEP PROBE IS NOT COMMITTED, and that is deliberate rather than an omission.** It lived in `gates/.probe/`, which `.gitignore` carries for exactly this. A script under `gates/` that no suite runs is what `jobs/a-test-in-the-repo-that-no-suite-runs-2026-09-28` exists about, and #497 set the same precedent for the same reason. The predicate above is stated in full so the sweep can be re-derived; what is permanent is the gate.

### TC-R64 (US-R72) — the lesson board comes back to its own size after a transient shrank it

TC-R64  US-R72 (#510), a transient piece of in-flow content must not cost lesson board width for the rest of
the visit.  GATE `gates/pending/75-fit-loop-one-way-ratchet.js` (RENAMED from `75-lesson-demo-board-latch.js` mid-run, see the gate's own header: the ratchet was measured on PLAY and PUZZLE too, so a lesson-scoped name would have certified one screen of four), **64 assertions over 10 browser launches** —
4 geometries x (clean + small-transient) arms plus a LARGE-transient arm at each of Kunal's two.  **THE COUNT WENT 26 -> 30 -> 64 ACROSS TWO ANTAGONIST
VETOES AND THE COMPOSITION MATTERS MORE THAN THE TOTAL:** antagonist A had two assertions REMOVED (they would
have reddened a correctly fixed bundle) and four added, and antagonist B's veto added a LARGE-transient arm at
each of Kunal's geometries, which is what turned block D from decoration into a discriminating control. GEOMETRIES: **320x540 and 375x568** (the two cells
where the latch reproduces; 320x540 is not invented here — `gates/regress/48-lesson-flow.js` already drives
it, labelled "inside the overflow band, not at its floor", #424) and **375x761** (Kunal's phone, R19's settled figure) plus
**375x730** (the legacy `lib.js` GEOS entry), driven at a SMALL transient as the control that a sub-threshold
transient is absorbed AND at a LARGE one (88px, 120px) where the board latches on his own phone.
INPUTS: the Endgames lesson at index 0 driven to its demo end by `gates/drive/lesson.js` state
`endgame-demo-end`, then `✋ Now I'll try it`; the transient is a 16px `flex:0 0 16px` div appended to
`#root`'s single in-flow child, which is the trigger the owning job's own #468 block established and the only
one anybody has been able to cause on demand.

PASS CONDITIONS, each failing separately: (a) **THE BOARD COMES BACK** — the practice board after the
injection cycle EQUALS the practice board with no injection, same geometry, same run (B1); (b) the two arms
AGREE AT THE DEMO END, before either diverges, which is B1's comparability precondition (A4); (c) the
injection MOVED the quantity the shrink branch reads, sampled per frame (A5); (d) the practice state was
actually reached in both arms, at every geometry (A0, A0b, D0b); (e) a board is painted in both arms (A1,
A1b, D0, D0c); (f) the clean board is HEIGHT-bound at the latch cells and WIDTH-bound at Kunal's (A2, D1);
(g) at Kunal's geometries the board is unmoved by the injection cycle (D2).

**BLOCK D'S PHASE LABELS SHARE A TASK WITH THEIR ACTIONS SINCE #528** (`jobs/gate-75s-d4-reddens-under-suite-load-on-mains-own-bundle-so-any-push-can-be-reddened-by-a-commit-that-cannot-have-caused-it-2026-10-21`). The label was written in one `page.evaluate` and the click, injection or removal in the next, so under suite load a frame of the PREVIOUS phase's board was sampled under the new label. #527's red, over main's own bundle, was one practice frame of 236.39, the DEMO board's width at 320x540, which is what that race samples. MEASURED by the orchestrator in a scratch clone of #527's tree, three busy loops on two CPUs: the two-step order put a wider frame in 7 of 20 runs at 320x540 and 3 of 10 at 375x568, each at the first practice index (one run, the first three) and each equal to that geometry's demo board (236.39, 270.88); the one-task order, under more load, 0 of 20 and 0 of 10; idle, both 0. The assertion is unchanged and must still fail on a bundle built to fail it (the recipe at the top of block D in the gate file); the landing run reruns that control.

**THERE IS NO EXPECTED BOARD WIDTH ANYWHERE IN THIS CASE, AND THAT IS THE DESIGN.** Block B drives the same
path twice in one run and compares the two results. Every absolute board pin in this suite is a function of
the container's fonts — `gates/lib.js`'s #480 note measures the app's stack and bare sans-serif 53.84px apart
on one line, and six consecutive containers once could not gate any tree because of it — so a relation
between two measurements taken in one run does not depend on any of that. **BUT THE WORD "IMMUNE" IS WRONG
AND WAS IN THE FIRST DRAFT [R18]:** the ASSERTION is container-independent and its ability to FAIL is not.
The latch step at 375x568 fires on an `over` of 2.88px, so a container whose lesson text lays out 3px shorter
makes that cell stop reproducing and B1 would go green on a broken bundle with nothing saying so. That is
what A5 exists for — it measures the peak `over` the injection produced, so a vanished margin reports itself
rather than passing quietly. Found by antagonist A.

**TWO ASSERTIONS WERE REMOVED BECAUSE THEY WOULD HAVE VETOED THE FIX THEY EXIST TO ENABLE.** The first draft
asserted `over===0` and `spacers===0` as PASS conditions, on the reasoning that a fix which returns the
pixels while leaving the loop blind to slack has not closed the class. That is backwards: the surviving
candidate fixes make `over` signed or widen the `spacers` predicate, so both assertions would have gone RED
ON A CORRECTLY FIXED BUNDLE. They were also a COPY of the app's predicate rather than a reading of it, so
their messages claimed something about `chess.jsx` the gate cannot see. They are now an `L.note` printing the
same numbers every run. A note is not a guard — and here the property is a fact about the implementation
under test, not a requirement on it, and the requirement is B1.

EVIDENCE IT FAILED BEFORE ANY FIX, which is what this register requires: run against **main's own bundle
`227126b82b81`**, the gate scores **60 pass / 4 fail**, real exit 1, and the four reds are the recovery
assertion at each cell whose transient exceeds that geometry's threshold — 320x540 at 16px (lostPx 24.00),
375x568 at 16px (16.00), 375x730 at 88px (29.20) and 375x761 at 120px (32.80) — and nothing else.

**AND THE GEOMETRY CLAIM THE FIRST DRAFT MADE IS WITHDRAWN IN FULL [R18].** It read "EXACTLY 0px at 375x730
AND 375x761 ... the defect is NOT on Kunal's phone", and it asserted that in the gate. It drove ONE transient
size, 16px, and published the result as a property of those geometries. The size is an input axis: at 375x730
the loss is 0.00 at 16, 56 and 64px and **29.20px at 88px**; at 375x761 it is **32.80px at 120px**. The app's
own #436 comment carried the qualifier the draft dropped. Antagonist B's veto; reproduced by the build before
acceptance. **THE LOSS ALSO SCALES:** at 375x568 an 88px transient lands on **192.00, losing 78.88px (29%)**,
which is the floor gate 48 records from the wild — so "16-24px" was an artefact of the probe size.

THE MECHANISM FIGURES, CORRECTED FROM THE FIRST DRAFT AND THE CORRECTION IS THE USEFUL PART [R18]. At 320x540
the shrink branch consumes `over=12.39` and spends `ceil(12.39/8)*8+8 = 24`, matching the loss exactly. **At
375x568 the first draft cited `over=6.88` and it is the WRONG INPUT:** 6.88 occurs at board 270.88 and drives
270.88 → 254.88, which `control.log` shows happens **on the clean arm too** (the clean demo→practice step is
a real 16px there), so it drives the LEGITIMATE step and not the latch. The latch step is **254.88 → 238.88**
and its input is **`over=2.88`**, which was in the same log and was never cited; `ceil(2.88/8)*8+8 = 16`, the
right answer from a different input. The first draft also cited the chain 270.88 → 254.88 → 238.88, which
loses 32px and not the 16 its cited spend produces. **This is the identical confound the same build correctly
caught in the owning job's own published figure, committed two paragraphs later in its own commit message.**

AND THE CONTROL CORRECTED THE OWNING JOB'S PUBLISHED FIGURE BEFORE IT WAS TRUSTED. The job publishes
"236.39 → 212.39", which compares a DEMO board against a PRACTICE board; the clean demo→practice step is 0px
at 320x540 and a real 16px at 375x568. So the latch is 24 and 16, and the job's number is right at 320x540
and would have overstated it by 16px at the geometry in its own title.

NOT CHECKED, named rather than claimed: the other three screen families the loop runs on (play, puzzle
browse/online, compact review) — the arithmetic half of the mechanism is screen-independent and the
instrument-blindness half was measured on the lesson screen only; the real-world trigger, unidentified by
every lane that has looked; and the four-geometry control was run on main's bundle at TWO geometries only —
the 0px at 375x730/761 comes from this gate's block D, which is a different instrument from the probe, and
the first draft's "at four geometries on both bundles" conflated the two [#411/#412].

register's own next-free sentence. **THAT SENTENCE SAID TC-R51 AND IT IS CORRECTED IN PLACE ABOVE, in the
paragraph that now opens "the next free id after this row is NOT TC-R51 - that claim is WITHDRAWN here".**
Measured 2026-10-07 by the stronger method that sentence itself prescribes - `git grep -o -E 'TC-R[0-9]+[a-z]?'`
over `claude` and `gates` across **all 84 refs under refs/remotes/origin**, not against main alone - every id
through TC-R54 was already in use on some ref, and TC-R51 was among them. It is ALSO reserved, separately, by
`jobs/three-build-rows-carry-an-epoch-integer-finishedat-a-status-outside-done-running-or-a-duplicated-build-number-2026-10-04`,
whose own text records that its other reservation, TC-R52, had already collided the same way. So TC-R51 would
have been the third id in this register minted onto a collision, and the collision was waiting in exactly the
place the previous two were found.

**AND THE BRANCH READING HAS SINCE BEEN OVERTAKEN BY MAIN, WHICH STRENGTHENS THE CHOICE RATHER THAN WEAKENING
IT [R18, re-derived 2026-10-08T04:0xZ against origin/main 41894d6].** When this row was cut on 2026-10-07 the
objection to TC-R51 was that it was taken *on a branch* - which is what this payload's own commit subject still
says, and that subject is now understated. **#496 HAS SINCE TAKEN TC-R51 ON MAIN**, in commits 48c5ffa and
80f2d6b, for the promotion-picker case that sits immediately above this note. So a run that had followed the
old next-free sentence would not merely have collided with a branch; it would have collided head-on with a case
already shipped in the section above. The gap id was right for a weaker reason than the one that now holds.

TC-R32 was chosen because it is free on **all 84 refs** and unreserved in the tracker's jobs collection, both
checked rather than assumed; the gap ids 33, 34, 38 and 40 are free on the same evidence, while 36 and 39 are
reserved by open jobs and are NOT free despite the superseded sentence having listed them.

**WHAT THIS CASE CANNOT SEE, said plainly because its own subject is an absence claim.** Neither arm reads the
repository, so neither can be run by `gates.sh` or by any gate, and a build that ships with the register
bi-typed goes green on both. Arm A cannot see a `priority` that is numeric and absurd (0, or 900): it checks the
TYPE and not the range. And it cannot see the nine rows with no `priority` key, which is why those are
published in the expected cell as a count rather than left to the arm.

## The tiered push gate itself - does gates/fastgate.sh accept a wrong input? Executed against #523 (`gates/fastgate-drive.sh`, wired into gates.sh's unit layer; log `gates/logs/<N>-fastgate-drive.log`)

**WHY THESE EXIST.** `gates/fastgate.sh` decides whether a commit may reach main WITHOUT the full suite, which
took 107 minutes at #514 and 111 at #522. Nothing in this repository tested it: `gates.sh` enumerates the suite
as `for f in "$G"/regress/*.js`, no regress gate reads it, and it has no `gate-manifest.tsv` row - so
`GATES GREEN` said nothing about it, and #522 landed 291 lines of it under a green suite that read none of them.
Job: `no-test-drives-gates-fastgate-sh-end-to-end-so-a-same-length-wrong-stamp-and-a-planted-register-row-both-take-a-fast-green-2026-10-15`.

**THE IDS START AT 057 AND THE JOB'S OWN POINTER NEEDED CHECKING [R18].** Its `startHere` says "TC-SUITE-056 is
the highest id in use", and in THIS FILE the highest is TC-SUITE-019 - 056 is published by
`gates/audit/cited-not-run.sh` and `gates/audit/r19-phone-geometry.sh` and appears nowhere in the register,
which is the same "published but unregistered" class #521's pen note records against TC-SUITE-RG-001. Measured
across the whole repository (`grep -rhoE 'TC-SUITE-[0-9]{3}'`), 056 IS the true ceiling, so the pointer is right
about the id space and this file is the thing that is incomplete. 057 cannot collide on either reading.

**AND SINCE #524 EVERY LINE THE HARNESS PRINTS CARRIES ITS TC ID.** Until then these ids appeared in this
register and in NO LOG: `grep -c 'TC-SUITE-0[567]'` on a gate log was 0, so no cell here could be checked
against an artefact and a reader had to map H4 to 060 by hand out of 1,270 lines. One `tc()` table in
`gates/fastgate-drive.sh` now supplies it to `expect()`, `expect_env()` and the hand-rolled `ok`/`SKIPPED`
lines, so the generated `PASS fastgate-drive ...` lines in the suite log carry it too. An unmapped case prints
`TC-SUITE-UNMAPPED(<case>)` rather than a blank, because a silently empty field is how a register and a log
drift apart. Side effect on arm (2), AND THE FIGURE THIS PARAGRAPH FIRST PUBLISHED IS WITHDRAWN [#524, both antagonists
reported it independently]. `verify-log.sh --citations` arm (2) counts an id published in the register and
absent from the log as unsupported, and this read "#523's thirteen rows ratcheted that counter by +13 (17
supported / 22 unsupported before, 17 / 35 after)". **I never measured the "before".** It was arithmetic on a
number I had seen once, which is the thing this file exists to stop. What IS measured, one command, on this
tree at 2026-10-16T01:20Z: `bash gates/verify-log.sh --citations` prints **17 supported, 32 unsupported, 11
misfiled under a section citing another log, 8 not checked**. The delta against any earlier tree is NOT stated
here, because stating it needs the same command run on that tree and I did not run it. Arm (2) is red either
way, so nothing green turned on this - what turned on it is whether a reader can reproduce the number, and
with a bare delta they could not.

**EVERY CASE ASSERTS THE EXIT CODE AND THE VERDICT LINE.** Exit code alone is not enough and that is measured
rather than argued: TC-SUITE-061's first fixture computed its date with `date -d 'TZ="America/New_York" <base> - 1 day'`,
which GNU date does not evaluate as it reads - it returned a date in the FUTURE - so the case asserted exit 2,
GOT exit 2, and was exercising TC-SUITE-060's rule instead of its own. It failed on the verdict line.

BOTH of #526's blind antagonists found that wrong, independently and from different doors, and the build reproduced it before changing a word: the operative quantity is the bytes still UNWRITTEN WHEN `grep -q` EXITS, and the check measures the length of the WHOLE list, which comes apart from it because padding placed BEFORE the target inflates the total while adding nothing after it. MEASURED: 499 long paths, the target at line 500, then 4501 short ones is **66,273 bytes over 5001 paths with the target inside the first tenth, so it SATISFIES ALL THREE CHECKS**, and it does not reliably fire the race at all. **THE FIGURE TO QUOTE IS THE DETERMINISTIC ONE AND IT IS NOT THE TRIAL COUNT: bytes-after-the-target is 43,903 against the 128,893 of the 5000-pad fixture committed until the #529 review (154,893 at its 6000 pads since)** - a property of the fixture that does not move. **THE RACE RATE IS LOAD-DEPENDENT AND IS NOT PUBLISHABLE AS A POINT FIGURE**, which `gates/fastgate.sh:1464` already records about this same mechanism ("It is LOAD-DEPENDENT - a quiet box missed 0 in 150"): across four fixtures and repeated runs of the same 40 trials the middle band read 0, 6, 7, 11, 11, 12, 20, 25, 26 and 29 of 40, and antagonist A withdrew its own "7 of 40" after re-reading 26, 29 and 25 on the identical fixture. Quote a direction and the command, never a number. **A FOURTH CHECK, on the length after the target line, is BUILT SINCE #529** (`jobs/h24s-byte-check-measures-the-whole-list-so-a-fixture-passing-all-three-self-checks-still-misses-the-race-2026-10-20`): it counts every line after the target with its newline **against 131,072** [#529 review, reviewer A's F2, by strace]: grep's one read asks for 98,304 bytes against a 65,536-byte pipe, so the read that finds the target can drain a full pipe, and the writer is forced to block, and so to take SIGPIPE, only when more than 65,536 bytes remain after it; between 65,537 and 131,072 the verdict is a timing race. The candidate first bounded the check at 65,536 and said it closed the race, which it did not: the 5000-pad fixture it then committed (128,893 after the target) passed all four checks inside that band. So the fixture is 6000 pads since (154,893 after the target: 6000 lines of 22 bytes plus 22,893 digits, and the case prints the figure on every run), and the 499-long-paths fixture (43,903) and the 5000-pad one both print `FAIL H24` naming their length (measured by #529, build__1792598873000 at 2026-10-21T16:17Z, on its tree after P1: over the committed fixture `bash gates/fastgate-drive.sh fixed` printed `H24 fixture: 154893 bytes after the target line, 6001 paths, target at line 1, list 154916 bytes` and `ok H24 [TC-SUITE-081] (exit 1, an honest mint would have made it)`; with the fixture built as mutant M10 builds it, 499 long paths before the target, it printed `FAIL H24 [TC-SUITE-081] only 43903 bytes of the changed list come AFTER the target line, at or under 131,072`; with the fixture cut back to 5000 pads, as mutant M21 cuts it, `FAIL H24 [TC-SUITE-081] only 128893 bytes of the changed list come AFTER the target line, at or under 131,072`; the orchestrator's rehearsal of the same patch read the same four lines); the three proxies stay, each naming a different way a fixture goes wrong. The limit this cell carried as FILED-not-fixed is now FIXED: position is itself a proxy, the true variable is how many bytes remain unwritten when grep exits against the 64 KiB pipe buffer, and the #525 re-read slipped a fixture past both proxies - `h/N.md`, 5001 lines but only 48,917 bytes, target at line 1 - which left H24 green in **4 of 6** runs against a fully broken script, while the committed `zzz-drive-pad` fixture (128,916 bytes) reddens it **4 of 4**. The byte assertion is what tells those two fixtures apart, and the proxies cannot. **MEASURED AT #526:** the committed fixture still prints `ok H24` (the list is 128,916 bytes, well over the buffer), the re-cut `h/` fixture now prints `FAIL H24` naming the length instead of reporting `ok`, and the SAME re-cut fixture through the PRE-FIX H24 prints `ok` - so the fix is what changed the verdict and a shortened prefix is a loud FAIL rather than a silent green one rename away. **AND THE 38-OF-40 FIGURE ABOVE IS NOT CORRECTED, WHICH IS ITSELF THE POINT [#526]:** a reader re-running the committed shape today gets 39 to 40 of 40, and 38 is within this instrument's own noise [#527: this said 'inside the instability band above', and no band quoted above contains 38], so the earlier figure is neither confirmed nor contradicted and there is nothing to withdraw. #526's first draft of this cell did 'correct' it to 40, and the antagonist that raised the discrepancy withdrew that half of its own finding before the cell was committed. Recorded because the near-miss is the lesson: a figure inside the noise of its own instrument must not be restated as an error, and this project has already nearly 'corrected' a citation that was exactly right [#450]. The all-three-checks-pass fixture read 6 to 29 of 40 across three observers and repeated runs; even the committed shape read 39 once in three. Publish the poles and the command, never a middle-band number. **ON THE UNIT** [corrected #527]: `${#H24L}` returns the BYTE count in a POSIX locale, which is this container's, and the CHARACTER count under a UTF-8 one; either way the figure is never more than the real byte count, so the test can only be stricter than it reads, never looser. Either of two things makes the two equal today, and each is enough alone: the POSIX locale, and the paths, which git prints in ASCII (it quotes every other byte). `jobs/h24s-position-self-check-guards-a-proxy-so-a-shorter-pad-prefix-slips-past-it-2026-10-16` Re-observed at #534 in its fixed run, copied from gates/logs/534-all.log: `` PASS fastgate-drive H24 [TC-SUITE-081]  (exit 1, an honest mint would have made it) ``. |

| TC-SUITE-178 | US-BN-01 | A TAMPERED EXECUTED FILE PLUS ONE PLANTED PATH WAS ROUTED INSTEAD OF REFUSED (#537, the TAMPER job). THE POSE, driven by `gates/fastgate-drive.sh` H52 in its own throwaway clone: a records-only commit (one minted register row) whose premise is asserted green FIRST, then one comment line appended to `gates/verify-log.sh` - a file the tier EXECUTES - hidden with `git update-index --assume-unchanged` so `git status` prints nothing, then one excluded untracked `gates/zz-h52-plant.js` added through `.git/info/exclude`. The case asserts TWO steps and both matter: the tamper ALONE must already refuse, or there is no refusal for a scan to get in front of and the case is not about ordering at all; and the tamper WITH the plant must still refuse, which is the defect. It also asserts the plant was still present at the moment of the probe, because a cleanup that ran early would make the second step silently identical to the first. | exit 1, FAST GATE REFUSED, naming `gates/verify-log.sh` and the two differing hashes, with the plant in place. MEASURED on a scratch clone of ed65bab carrying one records-only commit, which reads exit 0 FAST GATE GREEN: the tamper alone gives exit 1 FAST GATE REFUSED, and the tamper plus the plant gives exit 2 FULL SUITE REQUIRED naming only the planted file, with ZERO mentions of the tamper in its whole output. THE FIX IS PURELY THE ORDER OF TWO BLOCKS - the exit-1 tamper pin now runs before the two exit-2 untracked scans - and the reason it is a fix rather than a preference is that exit 2 is not a safe fallback here: the full suite then runs `gates/verify-log.sh`, which IS the tampered file, and it answers OK about itself. **AND FOUR MORE AT #544, FROM ITEMS (3) AND (4) OF THE SAME OPENING - THE TWO PUSH-CHECK SELFTESTS' OWN FIXTURES (208 to 211).** These are not the tier; they are the two selftests the tier runs, whose fixtures pinned shas that a shallow clone does not hold, so both files reddened in the one environment the build routine actually runs in. TWO OF THE FOUR RECORD SOMETHING THE OPENING DID NOT ASK FOR: 209, where the exit code alone is PROVED unable to fail - a mutant that deletes the register's object check leaves the case green, because the refusal falls through to the series guard - and 210, where the apparent intermittency is explained by the tool deepening the clone as a side effect of being tested in it, so the first run of the file fails and the second passes.

**ADDED AT #544, AND THESE ELEVEN ARE THE FIFTH TRY'S OWN CASES.** Four repairs, each with its own cases and its own mutant: the caller's proof of main (197 to 199), the read statuses (200, 201), the per-control origin (202, 203), the survivor set test (204, 205) and B's two smaller vetoes (206, 207). TWO OF THEM RECORD A LIMIT RATHER THAN A CLOSURE AND SAY SO IN THEIR OWN PASS CONDITION - 203, where a move-and-restore inside one control's own window stays unpinned, and 205, where the tie test is defeasible and what it buys is a ROUTE rather than a red. AND ONE MUTANT IS TWO-STAGE BECAUSE ITS ARMS OVERLAP (M57, on 200): mutating the entrance read alone reached exit 2 and not 0, since an unreadable index also fails the reads inside the snapshot, so a single-arm mutant would have proved less than its name claimed.


**WHAT THESE DO NOT COVER, named rather than left to be found.** H1b, the only case that rebuilds through
`gates/build.sh` for real, **SKIPPED on #523** because esbuild is not in this container; it prints SKIPPED and
does not count as an ok, so a container without esbuild cannot turn this green on fewer cases. The register
agreement rule (TC-SUITE-062/063) is checked against a `built` row only: a `shipped` row is deliberately not
accepted in its place, and that choice is untested here because no stamp-changing commit in main's history
relied on one. And the arm is only ever reached by a commit whose app.js diff is stamp-only AND whose every
other path is records: measured over all 1204 commits on main, 550 touch app.js, 43 are stamp-only, and only
TEN would reach this arm at all - so these cases govern a narrow door, which is the honest denominator.


**RESIDUALS (1) AND (2) BELOW ARE CLOSED SINCE #529, AND (2a) SINCE #533** (this heading read "AND (2a) IS NOT" until #533; leaving it would have made this paragraph say two things at once, which is the `aFIELDCONSISTENCYNOTE_516` shape) (`jobs/the-register-arm-of-fastgate-checks-the-numbers-of-appended-rows-and-not-the-rows-so-a-duplicate-or-wrong-bundle-row-takes-a-fast-green-2026-10-16`). The register arm now counts the appended rows with their multiplicity, so a byte-identical copy is in the difference and is refused (TC-SUITE-094); every appended row must carry nine fields and a state the tools write; and an appended `built` row must name a bundle this history holds, which refuses a records-only row naming a bundle nothing built (TC-SUITE-095) and still passes a held run's close-out (TC-SUITE-096); the first version of that rule let a forged row through four ways, which TC-SUITE-099 to 102 now refuse. What that cannot see: a held-trees row and a built row naming the same invented bundle pass together, and a row for a number the base carries is still not judged by its runId. (2a) **IS WHAT BUILD #533 CLOSES, AND THE SENTENCE THAT USED TO STAND HERE IS NOW FALSE:** it read "a gap fill still goes to a full suite that runs no series check". The full suite has a series door since #533 (`gates/gates.sh`, TC-SUITE-108 and 116-123), so a gap-fill row no longer reaches a green suite unjudged: every appended row at or below the base series top is judged by `gates/buildnum.sh stampable`, which answers from the row's own attribution with NO series top in the ownership path - which is why integrating origin/main cannot move the verdict, and is the one thing #530 (base top) and #532 (merge-base top) each got wrong. What the door still does NOT judge, declared rather than dressed up: a row AT OR ABOVE the base top is REPORTED by number, kind and `mintedBy` and not refused, so `gates/fastgate.sh`'s contiguity ceiling of 3 remains the stricter check on that class. Residual (3) is closed since #529 as well (`jobs/a-wrong-build-stamp-can-reach-main-on-an-app-changing-build-because-build-sh-does-not-validate-ct-stamp-time-2026-10-16`): gates/build.sh validates `CT_STAMP_TIME` before esbuild (TC-SUITE-097 and 098), and only a trial bundle under `CT_OUT` is let through, reported.

**ADDED AT #524, AND THE FIRST TWO ARE RESIDUALS OF THE BOUND THIS BUILD CHANGED.** (1) The appended-row set
difference is computed with `grep -vxF`, which compares rows as SETS, so **an appended row that is
BYTE-IDENTICAL to a row already in the base is invisible to it** - it is not in the difference at all, so no
case here covers it and the contiguity bound never sees it. Both of #523's antagonists measured this
independently and it survives the bound: contiguity constrains the NUMBERS that appear, and a duplicate row
introduces no new number. (2) The bound reads the number field only. A row whose number is contiguous but
whose **state, bundle or runId fields are wrong** - a `built` row naming a bundle that was never built, say -
is refused by nothing here; TC-SUITE-062 and 063 cover that for the STAMPED path only, where a bundle md5 is
available to match against, and a records-only commit has no such witness. **AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS
RESIDUAL UNDERSTATED IT** [#524, antagonist A]: it was written as though the number field were fully bounded,
and at the time it was not - every number AT OR BELOW the base top was skipped entirely, nine of which are
free gaps. That hole is now TC-SUITE-075 and the two doors are judged separately, so what remains is what
this sentence says: the non-numeric fields. (2a) AND THE NEW DOOR HAS ITS OWN RESIDUAL, named rather than
dressed up: **nothing distinguishes #454's legitimate census from a hand-written row claiming a free number.**
Both write a row at or below the top. The arm sends both to the full suite and names every number in the log;
it does not and cannot decide which one it is looking at - **and the second half of this sentence, which read "and the full suite runs no series check, so a census fill that reaches a green suite still lands", is FALSE SINCE #533** [antagonist B of #533, P2-6]. The fast arm still cannot tell the two apart, which is why it still sends both to the full suite; what has changed is what they arrive at. The suite's series door judges each such row by `gates/buildnum.sh stampable` and credits a census row only when its own fields say `census` (field 8) or the number is attributed to this run, so a hand-written row claiming a free number now reddens the suite instead of riding a green one. What the split buys is that neither takes the FAST door in
silence. (2b) The ceiling on new numbers ABOVE the top is **3**, and two things about it were wrong here until #527.
It is counted over the WHOLE DIFF from origin/main, not per commit, and it counts only the numbers that
`gates/held-trees.tsv` AT THE BASE does not vouch for: since #525 a held run's vouched mint is excluded. And 3 was
measured as the largest above-top count in one PUSH of main's history, which is #469, #480 and #517 (the code
comment at the ceiling has always named all three; this sentence named two), with #454's register-creating push
diverted by its own arm. It is therefore a bound on HISTORY, not on what is honest: a future
run that legitimately needs four - three branches merging their unlanded mints, say - will be refused and will
have to raise it with the measurement in hand. That is the right direction to fail in, and it is a frozen
denominator of exactly the kind this project files against itself, so it is written here where the next run
will look rather than only in the code. (3) `CT_STAMP_TIME` inside
`gates/build.sh` is still unvalidated, which is what makes TC-SUITE-060 and 071 reachable at all; the opening
for this run forbade touching it, and this tier is deliberately the second line of defence rather than the
first. (4) H10 and H11, the two controls `jobs/the-tier-guards-what-it-executes-but-not-what-it-reads-2026-10-14`
needs, for a working tree whose `49-home.js` regex is loosened or whose `require()` is removed under
`assume-unchanged`, did NOT exist at #524 (measured then by `grep -n 'H10\|H11' gates/fastgate-drive.sh` on the
held #523 tree and on #524's head). They exist from #527, which lands that job's read pin with them (TC-SUITE-085
and 086) and three more pieces of the same class: both pins hash with `--no-filters`, so a clean filter cannot make
a changed file read as committed (H10's second route for the read pin, H32 for the executable pin, TC-SUITE-090);
the premise check's existence tests ask the COMMIT whether a
path exists (H30 and H31, TC-SUITE-088 and 089); and a catch-all before the green hashes EVERY tracked file raw and
refuses unless each is the blob the index records (H28, TC-SUITE-087). **WHAT THEY STILL DO NOT COVER:** UNTRACKED
files. The catch-all hashes what git tracks, and `git status --porcelain` lists an untracked file only when no
ignore rule matches it (`.gitignore`, `.git/info/exclude`) and `status.showUntrackedFiles` is not `no`; the
premise's existence tests no longer consult such a file, but any other check that globs or reads the working tree
could still be fed one (measured before #527 landed by an independent reader: an untracked `gates/*.js` file matched
by `.git/info/exclude` and naming a dead gate log turned the citations ratchet RED on an honest commit, through
every guard here). And the catch-all runs LAST, so a doctored tree that an earlier check routes to the full
suite (exit 2) never reaches it; the full suite then runs on that working tree, which no check here compares with
the commit either. (5) THE TWO ROUTES OF
`jobs/part-2c-is-ordered-behind-the-stamp-arm-and-its-margin-accepts-ten-numbers-so-a-planted-register-row-still-lands-2026-10-15`,
named here because that job asks for them to be. **The ORDERING route** (an implausible stamp and a planted register row in
one commit, which used to reach the full suite at exit 2) is closed: the register arm's exit 1 wins, and H15 (TC-SUITE-072)
answers exit 1. **The MARGIN route** (a planted row within ten of the top, which used to be accepted) is closed for a row
that is NOT CONTIGUOUS with the top (H16 and H17 answer exit 1), and it is NOT closed for up to
THREE CONTIGUOUS rows above the top, because honest mints produce exactly that shape: H20 (TC-SUITE-077) is that accept
case and H19 (TC-SUITE-076) the refusal at four. Nothing in this arm can tell a planted contiguous row from a minted one,
since the row carries no proof of who wrote it beyond a runId anyone can type, and a contiguous BUILT row naming a bundle
never built is accepted the same way; that residual is
`jobs/the-register-arm-of-fastgate-checks-the-numbers-of-appended-rows-and-not-the-rows-so-a-duplicate-or-wrong-bundle-row-takes-a-fast-green-2026-10-16`.
